## 10. CHARACTERS THE ROOM INVENTED

### 10.1 WES ARCENEAUX — Lena's boyfriend at the opening 🔒
**Full name:** Wesley Joseph Arceneaux. Born **June 14, 1989** — 20. New Orleans (Gentilly). Jesuit High '07. Junior at
Tulane, civil engineering ("I'm going to build the walls right"). Lena's older brother Luc's best friend — four years
younger, he has followed Luc around since he could walk (the page's wording, S1E07 s08v; "since T-ball" retired after E07).

**Who he is.** During Katrina, at sixteen, Wes took his uncle's aluminum flatboat out on the third day and ran it through
Gentilly and the edge of the Ninth Ward for four days, pulling people off roofs and out of attics. He is a neighborhood
legend. He carried Lena's twin siblings out of the second floor of a neighbor's house. He has been in love with Lena
since she was fifteen, and he waited until she was seventeen to say so. They've dated since her senior-year Mardi Gras
(February 2009). They are "holding the line" together — his idea as much as hers — and they hold it: they never sleep together (hard rule). He's funny (a gentle, goofy, Saints-
obsessed humor), kind, devout in the way of a Jesuit boy: he can do the Ignatian Examen and the Who Dat chant in one
breath. He's the kind of boyfriend parents pray for.

**The unraveling (sad, not expected).** He is not cheating. He is not a cad. **He is losing God.** On the fourth day of the
boat, behind a house on Pauger Street, he heard knocking under a roofline — a woman in an attic — and couldn't get the
axe through the rafters before the water rose and his uncle made him go to the next house; when they came back in the
morning the knocking had stopped. He has never told anyone: not his uncle, not Lena, not a priest. Four years later,
studying the Army Corps of Engineers' failures in a Tulane classroom, he has begun to believe that the water was just
water and the knocking was just a woman and there was no one listening to either of them. He stops going to Mass in
September. He doesn't tell Lena. He drinks a little more each week. He keeps calling her every night at 10 Central / 8
Pacific, sweet and funny, and she keeps not noticing, because she doesn't want to.

**Jack and Wes.** **(E07:)** at dawn on the Delgado roof Lena tells Jack that Wes has bought a ticket — Columbus Day
weekend, "He wants to see the Pacific. I told him it's a threat." **(E08, after the Thérèse night — Lena turns toward him, seen
only through Jack's eyes:)** she calls *him* now, afternoons and late, from the east stairwell (2nd–3rd landing; her laugh carries
up); Thu Oct 1 she mails him a care package at the UCen counter while Jack stands in line behind her — a Mason jar of the Pacific
with sand in the bottom, the lid sealed with electrical tape, **THE PACIFIC. (A PREVIEW.)** in her RELIQUARY capitals, plus a
pound of See's and a Marisol sweatshirt; she argues the clerk out of "liquids" ("It's an ocean, not a liquid"). **Wes is thrilled**:
he calls her at six his time Saturday to say it came ("Luc says it smells like bait. Wes says it smells like me."); Sunday from
the Dome he texts in capitals: "WHO DAT 4-0!!! 5 DAYS. TELL THE OHIO GUY WHO DAT." 🔒 He means every word; the drinking is
still there; nothing about faith reaches the page. Wes visits Marisol Columbus Day weekend (S1E09). Jack expects to hate him and doesn't. They end up on the
beach at 2 a.m. with a bottle of Jameson; Wes tells Jack the attic story — the one he's never told Lena — because Jack is
a stranger, and you can tell a stranger. **(Revised for the timing tragedy:)** Wes tells it in the *past tense*, as a
wound that healed — "I couldn't kneel for a year after. Then she came along and I could" — and shows Jack a photo of
Mémère Odile's ring on his phone: he's going to ask Tony after Lena graduates. 🔒 It's half a lie; he is telling it to
himself. Wes says: "Take care of her. She thinks she's the one doing the carrying." Jack (and the player) come away
believing Wes is solid and the thing is forever; Jack knows something about Lena's boyfriend that Lena doesn't (the attic,
the ring), and it is the beach, the ring and that sentence that make him stop waiting — which is why, by Advent, he has
said yes to Becca. Neither Jack nor the player is shown that it's winding down until E21.

**The end (S1E21, Dec 12, 2009, Our Lady of Guadalupe).** On the east stairwell phone, Wes breaks up with her — gently,
devastatingly. Not because there's someone else. Because he can't kneel anymore, and he won't let her marry a man who
stands at the back of the church at their children's baptisms with his hands in his pockets. "You'd do it. You'd marry
me anyway. That's why I can't let you." She argues — she is impossible to out-talk — and she loses, because you cannot
argue someone into faith, and she knows it, and that is the worst thing she learns all year. Jack sits on the stairs
with her until dawn and does not kiss her. **He has had a girlfriend for two weeks.** (The tragedy of timing, brief.)

**After.** Lena keeps praying for him. Wes appears at Mardi Gras 2010 (S2) — sober-ish, kind, broken. 🔒 **He dies in
August 2011 (S5 opener)**, on the sixth anniversary of the storm, of an accidental overdose of oxycodone and alcohol. Jack
goes to New Orleans for the funeral. It's the hinge of the reconciliation. *(Death register: reconsider before S5 based
on the path; the Showrunner may instead let Wes live and return to the Church in adulthood.)*

**Voice.** Easy, slow, goofy, Gentilly-Y'at ("where y'at," "dawlin'," "yeah you right"). Talks about the Saints like
scripture. Underneath, long pauses.
- *"You're the Ohio guy. She said you were a jagoff. That means she likes you, I've known her since she had a retainer."*

### 10.2 SOL ARRIAGADA HOLM — Jack's magnetic girl 🔒
*(Rebuilt to the player's design, brief "Jack's two girls": from Theo's LA orbit; the fire without the faith.)*
**Full name:** Soledad Arriagada Holm. Born **September 11, 1989** (the 16th anniversary of the Chilean coup; her mother
calls her "my counter-revolution"). Raised in Los Feliz, Los Angeles. **A director's daughter and a former actress:** her
father, **Lars Holm**, a Swedish documentary cameraman turned studio director, has made three prestige thrillers for
Meridian Pictures (Richard Vance produced two). Sol played the middle daughter on a fictional cable family drama,
*Harbor Lights* (2001–05), from eleven to fifteen, and at seventeen starred in her father's one indie, which Meridian
distributed and which got her a Spirit nomination she didn't go to collect. She quit acting at eighteen. NYU Tisch film,
2008–10 (drops out); **transfers to Marisol as a junior in Film and Media, Fall 2010 (S3)**; a falling-down Las Olas
house on Del Playa with two surfers and a darkroom.

**Theo knows her.** Since they were twelve: Meridian holiday parties, premieres where they were each other's date, a
summer at fifteen they never discuss (nothing happened; that's the point). She calls him "Teddy" only to needle him; she is
the one person besides Richard who can. Theo knows exactly what she is — *"She collects saints, Costigan. She takes them
apart to see how they work, and she's very kind about it."* — and stays, as always, Team Lena.

**Who she is.** Fierce, intelligent, funny, sexy and utterly compelling. Surfs at dawn at the Point break; shoots Super 8
and 16mm; reads Neruda, Bataille, Duras, and Simone Weil (the last one secretly, and that's her crack). Sexually frank and
unashamed — not a party girl, an adult in a room of children. **Respectful of Jack's faith and mildly curious, but not
really interested in it**: she'll come to the 9 p.m. Mass once "for the light," and mean it about the light. Her flaws sit
exactly where Becca's strengths are: she won't try with his faith, won't go to Mass for him, won't pretend. Her mother,
**Isidora Arriagada**, a poet, was detained at Villa Grimaldi in 1975; the Catholic Church's Vicaría de la Solidaridad
got her out and to Sweden, where she met Lars. So Sol cannot dismiss the Church, and that maddens her.

**What she wants from Jack.** Precisely the thing he won't give her, and to understand why he won't. She is not trying to
corrupt him; she is trying to find out if he's real. She is the pull of the world at its most honest and beautiful.
**Hard rule:** they never sleep together. The arc lives at the line — kissing, making out, her darkroom, his resolve
cratering — and it is her incomprehension of the line, and his inability to make her see it, that ends them.

**The friends.** Laney is openly rude to her, like all the others, and privately, begrudgingly likes her (they smoke on
the Pi Lam roof and never mention it). **Lena can't stand her** — and doesn't tell Laney to be nice. Theo is polite and
Team Lena. Margot finds her "the only interesting person Costigan has ever dated, which is an indictment."

**Look.** Tall, long black hair usually wet from the ocean, sun-dark skin, a gap between her front teeth, a man's
steel watch (her father's), a Bolex camera, a wetsuit peeled to the waist, paint under her nails. A single silver earring.
Wears LA money like Theo does, as if it were cheap.

**Voice.** Spanish-inflected LA; switches to Spanish when she means it; direct to the point of rudeness; laughs at her own
jokes before she finishes them; questions as dares.
- *"You want me. I want you. Explain to me, slowly, with diagrams, why we're standing in this kitchen."*
- *"Your God got my mother out of a cellar. I'm not ungrateful. I'm just not his."*

**Arc.** **S1E12 glimpse:** up from LA for Las Olas Halloween with Theo's LA crowd, filming the chaos with the Bolex;
Theo sees her and says only, "Soledad. Don't." (Optionally at Caroline's in La Cañada, E13, if Jack goes.) S2–SU1:
glimpses in Theo's LA (a phone call; a premiere photo on Facebook). **S3 (Fall 2010):** she transfers in; teaches Jack to
surf; the relationship that keeps arriving at the line — Jack's lowest-resolve season. **Proxy fight #2** with Lena (S3E09,
§24.6). **The near-miss (S3E14, her darkroom):** it stops short — she stops it, when she sees he's going to hate himself in
the morning ("I don't sleep with men who'll confess me on Saturday"), or he does; resolve decides *how*, never *whether*.
Ends **S3E19** when she takes a second-unit job on her father's film in Chile — or when Jack ends it, depending on path.
Returns, a filmmaker, in S5, and once more in adulthood as a friend. Never a villain; she's right about how afraid Jack is.

### 10.3 PAIGE SATO-WHITMAN — RETIRED (after S1E06; folded into Becca, §10.4)
*(The tryer passes to Becca Lindell. The player met Becca on the page in E02 and E06 and responded to her as exactly the
tryer's blend — "sweet, faithful and without fire." Paige never reached the page. Her best pieces move to Becca: Coffee &
Donuts and knowing everyone's order; the color-coded Aquinas; the Five Ways learned by heart; the question that makes Osei
laugh; the hermit crab at the Blessing of the Animals; and a grandmother's rosary as a quiet mirror to Lena's never-asked
question (Becca's is the Sunshine Mine, not Manzanar). Do not use the name Paige Sato-Whitman for anyone.)*

### 10.4 BECCA LINDELL — Jack's "tryer" 🔒 (promoted after S1E06)
*(Brief, "Jack's two girls": the tryer, "the girl you bring home to mama"; a sincere, shallow cradle Catholic met at Newman,
the nicest girl there; the faith and the effort without the fire. **She is the first of Jack's two girls, in the
timing-tragedy slot.** Player note after S1E06: the player *assumes* Becca is the tryer and calls her "the exact blend we
were looking for of sweet, faithful and without fire." **Never confirm or deny it to the player; keep exactly that blend.**)*
**Full name:** Rebecca Anne Lindell. Born **May 31, 1991** (the Visitation) — 18. A cattle-and-hay ranch on the Rathdrum
road outside Coeur d'Alene, Idaho ("it's a lake, people think it's a cheese"). Homeschooled through twelfth grade (a parish
co-op at St. Pius X; 4-H; the county fair). Freshman, Biology, pre-vet ("large animal. Cows don't lie to you"). Portolá
Hall; her roommate is a nursing major from Spokane. Drives her brother Eli's red 1994 Ford Ranger with Idaho plates and a
Rathdrum feed-store sticker (she and her father drove it down in August).
**Family.** Father **Karl Lindell**, rancher, Knights of Columbus, says grace like he's reading a contract; mother **Marie**,
who ran the house and the homeschool and lights candles for everything (E06: "She's lighting a candle in Coeur d'Alene").
Seven siblings — five brothers, two sisters; ten at dinner; no TV; the oldest sister is the one you'd be scared of; Eli lost
a front tooth to a Benedictine, Father Anselm, at a church-picnic volleyball game. 🔒 **The rosary** she prays every night,
wrapped twice around her wrist at Mass, was her grandmother's — **Agnes Lindell**, whose husband Walt died in the **Sunshine
Mine fire at Kellogg, Idaho (May 2, 1972; ninety-one men)**. Agnes prayed it at the mine gate for the eight days it took,
and on the eighth day they brought two men up alive, and neither one was Walt. Becca tells this, when she tells it (S2E03),
as a sweet fact about a rosary; she has never asked her grandmother one question about those eight days. (A quiet mirror:
Lena has never asked her mother about Memorial; Nonno's Black Monday is a candle on Glenwood Avenue. Becca's not-asking is
sweetness; Lena's is a wound. Jack sees both, and the narration never says so.)
**Who she is.** Cute, sweet, warm, frank, funny in a dry, delighted way, much tougher than she looks — pulled a calf at
twelve, can butcher a deer, whistles a herd off a county road with no hands. Wants to be married by twenty-one and says so on
the second date; wants seven or nine children ("Odd numbers, so there's a tiebreaker"). Everyone at Newman thinks she and
Jack are perfect together; Mrs. Fitch, Tyler and the Coffee & Donuts table say "you two" by October. **A sincere, shallow
cradle Catholic:** Mass every Sunday of her life, the Glory Be when a cow is kicking her, her grandmother's rosary, Newman
like a family — and she has never wrestled with any of it. "I don't need it to be complicated. I need it to be true. It's
true. So."
**How she tries** (with his faith *and* his rigor): asks him to teach her the Five Ways and learns them by heart on a set of
bleachers, delighted and untroubled (E07); reads Aquinas with color-coded tabs from her clipboard; reads *Caritas in
Veritate* because he quoted it on a beach; comes to Hale's public lecture in her nicest cardigan; reads O'Connor and hates it
("Why is everybody so *mean*?"), reads Percy and loves it; asks Osei a question so good he laughs for a full minute (she has
no idea why it was good). Jack's arguments delight her and slide off. Asked if she ever thinks it might not be true: "No," —
honestly puzzled — "Do you?" She respects his line and does not test it; she is waiting too.
**Hard rule:** they never sleep together. Kissing, warmth, her head on his shoulder at the 9 p.m. Mass — and nothing deeper
there. That is the whole tragedy. **Never a placeholder** (brief): Jack already knows how he feels about Lena, so Becca must
have real gravitational weight of her own — she is the whole good life everyone wants for him (his mother, sight unseen:
"Seven! Catholic?"), and on some nights he wants it too; the player must be able to love her.
**The friends (brief).** **Laney hates her on principle** and is openly rude ("Is she Amish or just punctual?" — E07);
**Lena tells Laney to be nice — and means it, which is worse**; **Theo is flawlessly polite to her face and texts Laney her
schedule** (always Team Lena). Margot can't find anything to object to and finds that suspicious. Kevin likes her and tells
Theo and Laney to leave it alone.
**Look.** Five-seven, strong, sun-browned, a dark-blond braid as thick as a bell rope, sunburn along the part, freckles she
doesn't cover, forearms that have done things to hay, marker on her knuckles from lettering posters; Wranglers, Ropers, a
Carhartt vest, a spiral planner and the clipboard. Her scar story about anybody is the straight one.
**Voice.** Frank, unembarrassed, practical; ranch competence as comedy; scientific about feelings; never sarcastic; never a
joke at her faith.
- *"I've pulled a calf. Give it here."* · *"You can think about it. I'm patient. We had a whole talk."* · *"Glory Be. You can
  get through it while a cow's kicking you."*
**Arc.** E02 "Put me down" (the BBQ poster). **E06 date #1, retroactively** (the cleanup; on the played path Jack told her it
was "a beach cleanup *and* a date"; FRIDAY? on the clipboard; the Franciscan scar telling). **E07 date #2** (Fri Sep 25):
Marisol High's Friday-night football game in the Ranger; the First Way on the bleachers; "Do you ever think it might not be
true?"; a kiss on the cheek "so there's no confusion"; Laney's first rudeness in the Fishbowl. **E08 (revised after S1E07 for the
player's own two-hander design): date #3 is Jack's** — out of the guilt of the Thérèse night he plans it for the very next day,
with real effort, and **he asks and he pays** (Thu Oct 1 call from the east stairwell; Fri Oct 2): by path, **Nonna's Friday
sauce** cooked in the Newman House kitchen (Becca keeps Friday abstinence year-round "because my family does"; Nonna, on the
phone, remembers *venerdì* perfectly and asks about the girl twice), **the elephant seals at Piedras Blancas at sunset** in
Kevin's borrowed Odyssey (Lena's 3–0 in white polish on the back glass there and back), or **two rented horses on the dunes**
(a week of tips; Becca rides like she was born on one). **Becca is thrilled** on every path ("Nobody's ever planned a thing for
me. I'm the one who plans the thing."). She has prayed for "two in the morning" every night since the bleachers and says so (on
`e7_doubt=="twoam"`) — the night after he spent two in the morning on a beach with Lena. First seed of E10's crack, without
cruelty: "You're always somewhere else a little. That's okay. My dad's like that in calving season." **Sat Oct 3, the Blessing
of the Animals,** is a Newman event, not a date: she brings a hermit crab borrowed from the Marine Lab touch tank in a Tupperware,
named **Thomas** ("Like your First Way") — Laney: "Who brings a *crab*."; she asks Osei whether a borrowed crab's blessing is hers
or the state of California's, and he laughs for a full minute; Mrs. Fitch says "you two" in Lena's hearing. **E10 the first crack:** "You look at me like I'm homework." — and instead of ending it, she
stays: "Then do your homework, Jack. I'll wait." (The tryer tries; it makes it worse later.) E13–E14 Coffee & Donuts, his
order remembered. **E16** the *New Moon* night Laney forces on the floor — Becca loves it, sincerely; Laney is rude on
principle; Lena, in the row behind, tells Laney to be nice. **E19 (Advent): official** — "My mom's been calling you my
boyfriend since September. Can I tell her she's right?" — after Lena can't come to Youngstown and after Wes's ring. **E21:**
Wes ends it with Lena; Jack has had a girlfriend for two weeks. **S2 (Jan–Mar 2010):** the trying; the Sunshine Mine rosary
told as a sweet fact (S2E03); Lena's Gideon spring; **S2E15 (Lent):** after the Stations she tells him she loves him; he
can't say it back; she sees — without cruelty, because she is never cruel — that he has been loving her a little in order not
to love someone else, and names it: *"I did everything right. I know I did. It just isn't the thing, is it."* He has no
argument. Nobody is wrong. **After:** a friend; marries in the summer of 2011, at twenty — by twenty-one, as she said — a
Veritas missionary (room's call: Tyler Brandt is available), which Margot considers a hate crime; comes to Jack and Lena's
wedding (AD) with twins.

### 10.5 PRIYA RAMAN — the junior RA
**Born October 14, 1988.** Fremont, California. Junior, Biochemistry. Delgado Hall 4th-floor RA (Room 401). Three jobs
(RA, library circulation, organic chem tutor). Overwhelmed; laminated everything; "per my email"; color-coded door tags;
an air horn for emergencies. Parents run a motel in Fremont and believe she's pre-med. 🔒 She's quietly failing organic
chemistry and has changed her major on paper without telling them.

🔒 **Nora connection:** Priya was Nora Calloway's freshman roommate (Delgado 2007–08, Room 222) and her best friend that
year. They drifted sophomore year (Nora moved to Serra Hall with Sloane Kittredge, got secretive). Priya has a box of Nora's
things in the RA storage closet — the Calloways never collected it — including Nora's journal, which Priya has never been
able to open past the first page. Her rule "Nobody goes to the Point after dark" is personal. On Nora's birthday she buys two mylar balloons at the Safeway
(two for one), ties one to the cross before her 8 a.m. orgo lab, in daylight, and keeps the other in the 4th-floor lounge
with a grocery cupcake. Facebook keeps suggesting Nora to her residents (no memorial status exists until late Oct 2009); she
has reported it three times. She knows the Memorare by heart because Nora prayed it in the shower every morning (Priya is
Hindu).

**Voice.** Rapid, bureaucratic, accidentally hilarious, passive-aggressive in Comic Sans. "Per the Residential Life
Handbook." "I'm going to need you to not." When she breaks, she breaks completely, then re-laminates.
- *"Rule one: nobody goes to the Point after dark. That's not a Res Life rule. That's my rule. I will know."*

**Arc.** S1: comic relief who becomes a source (she tells Jack Nora's name, E04; shows him the box, E11). S2: opens the
journal. Later seasons: switches to public health, tells her parents (Diwali episode, S5).

### 10.6 RAFI OCHOA — the floor dealer
**Rafael Ignacio Ochoa.** Born **June 2, 1991**. Marisol townie (the Westside, near the harbor). Freshman, Environmental
Studies, on a Cal Grant and a surf-team spot. Delgado 204 (his roommate never showed; "the ghost of 204"). Discreet weed and
Adderall dealer: a tackle box of "vitamins," delivery by skateboard, calls everyone "boss," quotes his abuela and Marcus
Aurelius (his abuela gave him the *Meditations*). Mellow, polite, scrupulously honest about prices. 🔒 **His father is
Detective Sergeant Ray Ochoa of the Marisol Police Department**, who investigated and closed Nora Calloway's death as an
accident. 🔒 Rafi, then a high-school senior, sold Nora two Xanax bars at the Lighthouse party on April 25, 2009. He hasn't
sold bars since; he says, on Jack's first night, "Not bars, though. I don't do bars anymore." He doesn't know whether the
pills mattered. He's terrified that they did.
**(E06 plan, played path:)** Jack (living route in the pilot) never heard "Not bars" or saw the tackle box; E06 re-plants
both after his father's stop on Ocean Road: *"Weed. Adderall around midterms. Not bars, though. I don't do bars anymore."*
(🔒 Nora's two bars; no gloss.) Rafi skates; La Flaca was "reported abandoned at the harbor in 2007" (Ray's line, true).

**Voice.** Surfer-mellow, courtly, philosophical. "Boss." "No stress." "My abuela says the devil's patient, so be faster."
**Arc.** S1: supply and comedy; offers Jack money-for-errands during the money crisis (E15). S2: his father's case file
becomes a target. S4: his part comes out; his father's reaction is the best scene Ray Ochoa ever gets.

### 10.7 EVAN MARSH — the TA with secrets 🔒
**Born February 11, 1980.** Duluth, Minnesota. PhD candidate in Philosophy (dissertation on Kierkegaard and moral
injury, Hale advising, year seven, stalled). TA for PHIL 1's Thursday 4 p.m. section (Hollis 108) — Jack and Lena's
section. Lapsed Lutheran. Dry, exhausted, precise, kind to struggling students, savage to lazy ones.

**The secret.** He was Nora Calloway's boyfriend from October 2008 to her death. She was in his section; they told no one
(it would have cost him his funding and her her reputation). She was eight weeks pregnant. On April 25 they fought by
text; at 12:05 a.m. she texted him from the Point: *"I'm at the Point. Please come. I'm keeping it. I want to keep it. I
want you to want it."* He read it at 12:20 and drove. At 12:31 his headlights caught a tall blond kid running from the
Point path toward Seacliff Road clutching something dark. He went to the wall, saw nothing, called her phone, heard
nothing, assumed she'd left to punish him, and drove home furious. At dawn surfers found her. He told police he'd been
asleep and seen her text in the morning; he deleted it. He has spent four months hunting for a tall blond boy.

**What he does.** He is not a villain; he is a coward that night who is now trying to be brave in the wrong way. He sees
Theo at the Halloween chaos (S1E12) and goes cold. He begins asking careful questions (S1E19: "Your roommate — was he at
Preview Weekend last April?"). In S2 he becomes dangerous — to Theo, and to himself.

**Look.** Lanky, prematurely gray at the temples, wire glasses, a stained Carhartt jacket, a bike he rides everywhere
because he sold his car in the summer (🔒 he couldn't drive past the Point). Smokes American Spirits.
**Voice.** Minnesota-flat, dry, exact; Kierkegaard quotes; long silences. *"Purity of heart is to will one thing. I've
never managed two."*

**Arc.** S1: the man at the end of the Lighthouse driveway (pilot); the section; the questions. S2: the anniversary
confrontation. S3: dates Margot; she finds the text history he never deleted from his laptop. S4: tells the truth to the
police and to the Calloways — the pregnancy, the text, the blond boy. Clears Nora of the suicide whisper. Loses his PhD.
Later: teaches philosophy at a prison education program in Minnesota; writes Jack once a year.

### 10.8 JACK'S FAMILY — the Costigans of Youngstown
Home: a narrow two-story frame house on **Glenwood Avenue near Brownlee Woods**, South Side, with a detached garage where
Frank keeps a space heater, a radio and a Stroh's fridge. Parish: **St. Brigid of Kildare** (fictional), South Side. Pastor:
**Fr. Dom Bruno** (named E06 plan; sixties, skipped the homily on Ephesians 5 the Sunday before Jack left — Aug 23, 2009).

- **Francis "Frank" Costigan, 51.** GM Lordstown assembly, 26 years, UAW Local 1112. Laid off since the GM bankruptcy
  (June 1, 2009); unemployment and the last of the SUB pay are running out by October. Drives a **1999 Chevy Silverado**
  (forest green, rust over the rear wheel wells, 212,000 miles). Sings Sinatra in the garage. Kennedy Democrat, pro-life,
  union to the marrow. Funny in a gruff, minimal way. Loves his kids ferociously and can't say it. 🔒 **Frank knows Jack
  threw the punch.** He brokered the plea: he went to Danny in the county jail and Danny said "Jackie's got the
  scholarship, Dad," and Frank let him. He has chosen one son's future over the other son's freedom, and he has to look at
  both of them. He drove Jack 2,400 miles because he couldn't afford two plane tickets and because he wanted to see the
  Pacific once before he died. He sleeps in the truck on the way home to save the motel money.
  - *"Close your mouth. You'll catch flies."* · *"Don't waste it."* · *"We don't. Not here. That's done."*
- **Maria Teresa (Rinaldi) Costigan, 49.** Secretary at St. Brigid's school. Devout, fierce, funny, tired. Visits Danny at
  Trumbull Correctional every other Sunday after the 8 a.m. Mass. 🔒 **Does not know** the truth. Believes Danny's version
  (a fight that went wrong). Her grief over Danny is the family's weather.
  - *"Did you eat? Don't lie to me, I can hear it in your voice when you haven't eaten."*
- **Daniel Francis "Danny" Costigan, 22** (b. Feb 2, 1987). 🔒 Serving five years at **Trumbull Correctional Institution**
  (Leavittsburg, Ohio) for felonious assault, pleaded January 2008. Eligible for judicial release consideration in 2011;
  release date 2012. He took the fall for Jack. He is funny, profane, gentle, does push-ups and reads every book Jack sends
  him (Jack sends one a month, used, with his own margin notes erased because the prison won't allow writing — Jack writes
  new notes on a separate sheet). In his letters he calls Jack "Professor." He does not resent Jack. That's the worst
  part for Jack.
  **(E06 plan:) Danny's first letter on the page** (lined paper, block capitals for the address, TRUMBULL CORRECTIONAL
  INSTITUTION and his inmate number on the envelope — Researcher to confirm the return-address format), written **Wed Sep
  16 night after he called the house and Gina said "fine,"** mailed Thursday, in Jack's Delgado box **Saturday**. It crosses
  the Thursday call: *"Tell Gina it ain't hers to carry. I got it."* (🔒 he is carrying Jack's; dramatic irony, never
  glossed) — which is what the system cut off ("tell Gina — "). Also: *"Tell the veil girl thanks for asking my name.
  Nobody out there asks it anymore. They ask Ma how I'm doing like I'm the weather."* Reggie from Portsmouth; the Hopkins
  ("Ma says it's poems. Send it anyway"). Signed **—D.** Danny's written voice: run-on, profane in a clean way, funny,
  generous; no self-pity; one sentence that stops Jack cold per letter.
  **(E08 plan:) Danny's second letter** — his answer to Jack's Sept 21 letter (which gave him Lena's name), in the Delgado box
  **Tue Sep 29, Michaelmas**: opens "Happy St. Mike's, Professor" (🔒 Danny was his confirmation sponsor; never stated); Reggie
  has decided the Pacific smells like "money and fish, which is California"; *Brideshead* is still with the C.O. ("he says it's
  about a house"); Gina told him on the Wednesday call that "the veil girl" has a boyfriend — "Gina says a lot of things. Tell
  Lena hey anyway. Lena. I like knowing it." The sentence that stops Jack cold: **"You sound happy out there. Don't feel bad about
  it. Somebody in this family ought to be. That's an order."** (🔒 the man carrying Jack's cross absolving him in advance, in the
  week Jack is guiltiest about something else; never glossed.) The Hopkins (margins erased — *Show Lena* erased by Lena herself,
  in the Delgado laundry room, Wed night) and Hale's Housman ship to Trumbull through Driftwood Thu Oct 1; Jack's reply says the
  Housman is "from a man who doesn't sleep" (`e7_housman=="sleep"`).
  **(E08:) Frank** — the Tuesday union-hall meeting (Local 1112): a recall list and talk of the small car (the Cruze) "next year,
  maybe"; Frank on the extension Saturday: "We'll see." (Researcher: keep to the public 2009 record — the Cruze was slated for
  Lordstown; no dates promised on the page.) **Rosie turns eight Wed Oct 7**; she wants "a crab" (her E06 drawing); Jack mails
  her a card Thursday.
- **Regina "Gina" Costigan, 15** (b. Apr 9, 1994). Sophomore at Ursuline High School. **(E05 canon:)** in sophomore
  religion, during the unit on the corporal works of mercy ("visit the imprisoned"), **Ashley Vrabel** says Gina's the class
  expert on the imprisoned; the room laughs; the teacher says "Ladies" and moves on. Ashley's father got a DUI last
  Christmas (South Side gossip; nobody was hurt — 🔒 the lucky driver). Smart-mouthed, Facebook-native,
  texts Jack constantly in lowercase. Bullied at school as "the convict's sister" (S1 subplot). Jack's favorite person to
  argue with. **(E07 plan:)** knowing the nun's name since E06, she friend-requests "Magdalena Broussard" on Facebook; Lena
  accepts and writes on Gina's wall (signed —L.); Gina finds Lena's "In a Relationship with Wesley Arceneaux" and Becca's
  two-exclamation-point post on Jack's wall, and says the obvious in lowercase. She does not tell Nonna. **Maria (E07, the
  Saturday call):** learns "Idaho" from Gina (Gina showed her Becca's profile picture) and is delighted ("Seven!"); then asks
  after the veil girl.
- **Rosaria "Rosie" Costigan, 7** (b. Oct 7, 2001 — Our Lady of the Rosary). Makes Jack drawings and mails them. Thinks
  California is made of beaches and Hannah Montana.
- **Nonna — Carmela (Ferraro) Rinaldi, 84.** Born in San Giovanni in Fiore, Calabria; came over 1951. Widow of Nonno
  Antonio Rinaldi, a Youngstown Sheet & Tube man who lost his job on **Black Monday, September 19, 1977**, and never
  recovered. Lives with the Costigans. Prays the rosary for Jack by name. Slips him twenties. Calls him "Giacomino."
  Early dementia beginning in S1 — **first on the page in E06** (Sat Sep 19, the phone): she asks *"E la ragazza?"* about
  the girl, gets her answer, and five minutes later asks it again in the same words, as new; Maria takes the phone back:
  "She's tired, honey." No gloss; Jack notices and tells only Lena (two-hander). 🔒 **Nonna knows.** She saw Jack's hand the morning after — the split knuckles, the
  stitched eyebrow, the blood under his nails — and said nothing. In S1E18 (Thanksgiving, St. Elizabeth's hospital) she says to him, in Italian, *"Tuo fratello
  porta la tua croce"* — your brother is carrying your cross. 🔒 Nonna dies in S3 (Lent 2011? or Advent 2010 — decide by
  path); Lena comes to the funeral in Youngstown.
  **(E08 plan:)** Maria told her the veil girl has a boyfriend (`e7_veil=="boyfriend"`): "Peccato." Then, sharp as a tack about
  the one thing: **"E l'altra?"** — and the other one? (Idaho.) Her memory for the Church calendar is perfect while the rest
  slips: on the Friday-sauce path she gives Jack the meatless *sugo* for a Friday ("Venerdì! Niente carne!") step by step, then
  asks who it's for, twice.

### 10.9 LENA'S FAMILY — the Broussards of New Orleans
Home: a raised double shotgun converted to a single on **N. Dupre Street, Mid-City**, near Bayou St. John. Took 4½ feet
of water in 2005; rebuilt by Tony and his brothers by hand over two years; the waterline is still visible on the shed.
Parish: **Our Lady of the Holy Rosary** (Esplanade Ave.) — the family pews; Lena's First Communion; the Christmas Eve
crawl. Family business: **Broussard's Seafood**, a market on Banks Street.

- **Antoine "Tony" Broussard, 53.** Owns Broussard's Seafood. Loud, sentimental, a world-class griller and grudge-holder,
  Saints season-ticket holder since 1981 (through the Aints years, with a bag on his head in 1980 — family lore). Cajun
  roots in Breaux Bridge; talks to God like a business partner who owes him money.
  - *"Cher, you call your mama or I'm flying out there and I'm bringing the boudin."*
- **Celeste (Guidry) Broussard, 50.** Registered nurse; in 2005 an ICU nurse at **Memorial Medical Center** (Uptown);
  now at Touro Infirmary. Brisk, funny, ferociously competent, gives orders in a whisper. 🔒 **Her mother, Lucille Guidry
  (Lena's grandmother, "Grandmère"), was a long-term acute care patient on the hospital's seventh floor during Katrina, and
  was among those who died there before evacuation.** Celeste was working in the ICU downstairs. On the fourth day she was
  ordered onto a boat with other staff, and went, believing her mother would be evacuated within hours. She was not.
  Celeste has never told her family what she knew or didn't, what she asked or didn't ask, what she was told. The
  coroner returned Lucille's Miraculous Medal in a plastic bag in February 2006; Celeste gave it to Lena at fifteen
  without a word. Lena has never asked. Sheri Fink's long investigation of the Memorial deaths was published in *The New
  York Times Magazine* in late August 2009 — **the week of the pilot.** Celeste texts Lena: *"Don't read the Times this
  week, cher."* 🔒 *Writers' rule:* the show never asserts what any real person at Memorial did or did not do. The
  Broussards' grief is about **uncertainty, absence and a daughter's choice**. Celeste's own culpability, if any, is the
  choice to leave on the boat. The truth Celeste finally tells (adult seasons): she went back up the stairs before she
  left, and her mother was asleep, and she kissed her and left her, and she has wondered every day since if her mother
  ever woke.
- **Luc Broussard, 24.** Eldest; NOPD officer (joined 2006). Wes's best friend. Quiet, good, under pressure: in 2010 the
  federal Danziger Bridge indictments and other post-Katrina NOPD scandals tear at his department. 🔒 He knew things
  about his precinct's conduct in the storm's aftermath that he never reported — a mirror for Lena (S2–S3).
- **Thérèse "Tess" (Broussard) Fontenot, 22.** Married at 21 to Beau Fontenot, a lineman for Entergy; first baby due
  December 2009 (a girl, **Lucille**, born Dec 19, 2009 — Lena becomes godmother over Christmas break).
- **Remy Broussard, 20.** At LSU; party boy; Tiger Stadium; adores Lena; the family's comic disaster.
- **Jude and Josie Broussard, 13.** Twins. Wes carried them out of a neighbor's second floor in 2005. Jude is quiet; Josie
  is Lena in miniature.
- **Mémère — Odile Broussard, 88.** Tony's mother, Breaux Bridge, speaks Cajun French first, makes the best gumbo in
  Louisiana, thinks Jack is "un bon garçon mais trop maigre."

### 10.10 CALEB HARGROVE — Hale's acolyte (added after S1E01)
Born **March 3, 1991** — 18. Visalia (Tulare County). Homeschooled until tenth grade, then Redwood High '09. Philosophy.
PHIL 9H. Portolá Hall.
**Who he is.** A Pentecostal (Assemblies of God) pastor's son, raised on tongues and the King James ("Selah," "verily," by
reflex). Deconverted at sixteen reading *The Consolations of Disbelief*; now Hale's most devoted reader — forty Post-its, a
signed copy, a Darwin fish on his laptop. Fast, funny, a little desperate, generous like a puppy; does tongues at parties as
a bit and looks sick afterward. 🔒 **Why he left:** at fifteen his father's church collapsed when the treasurer emptied the
building fund; the congregation scattered; his father's faith stayed untouched and serene, and Caleb could not forgive the
serenity (Hale's own wound a generation down; Hale will recognize it and use it). 🔒 He wrote Hale **four letters of
devotion; Hale never answered** — Hale writes only to believers who fight him. Caleb learns in S1E02 that Hale answered
Jack, and in S1E14 that he answered Lena. **Arc (MS):** S1 the boy Hale's book worked on, Jack's comic shadow; S2 Lena beats
Hale at the Forum and something in Caleb cracks; S3 he turns up at Laney's hospital room with a deck of cards; S8 (open) the
Easter Vigil — a back pew, gone before the Gloria. Never a strawman: his unbelief is honest grief, and he's right that his
father's church was a con for some of the people in it.
**Voice.** Central Valley twang, rapid, King James cadence used ironically; self-deprecating; "dude" in moments of terror.
- *"He wrote you back? I wrote him four letters. Four. I used a thesaurus on the third one."*

### 10.11 YUSUF NOORZAI — the third believer at the table (added after S1E01)
Born **November 12, 1990** — 18. Fremont, California (Little Kabul). Mission San Jose High '09. Aerospace engineering; PHIL
9H as his honors GE. Cabrillo Hall. Afghan-American, Sunni, devout and unshowy: finds a quiet stairwell for the prayers,
fasts Ramadan (Aug 22 – Sept 19, 2009 — he's fasting through Hale's first seminar), doesn't drink, plays cricket on the
Commons with grad students on Sundays. Dry, kind, precise; calls Jack and Lena "the Catholics"; Margot calls the three of
them "the Abrahamic bloc." Father **Ahmad**, a 1985 refugee by way of Peshawar, works the line at **NUMMI**, the GM–Toyota
plant in Fremont: GM pulled out in June 2009 and Toyota announced in late August it would close the plant in spring 2010
(Researcher to verify). Two sons of auto workers at Hale's table. Mother **Farida** runs a home daycare. Older brother
**Tariq**, 24, is a contract interpreter with the US Army in Helmand. 🔒 Tariq is wounded by an IED in late November 2009;
Yusuf gets the call the week of the West Point speech (S1E19). **Arc (SEA→MS):** S1 a friend at the table; E04 the
health-care night; E19 Tariq; S2 NUMMI closes (April 2010). The only arguments in the show where three people agree on
the first premise. Never a token: his own jokes, his own doubts (angrier at the Taliban than at God, and at America for
leaving the first time).
**Voice.** Bay Area understatement; deadpan; "inshallah" only when he means it; engineering metaphors ("load-bearing,"
"factor of safety"); a running bit about Catholic potlucks.
- *"I'm fasting, Professor. My soul is the only part of me that isn't hungry."*

### 10.12 RUDY SALCEDO — the Anchor's line cook (added after S1E01)
Born **March 30, 1966** — 43. Oxnard, then Marisol's Westside. Six years at Soledad (Correctional Training Facility) for
the armed robbery of a liquor store at nineteen (1985–1991; a starter pistol, a pint of Bacardi in him, nobody hurt).
Sober since 1994 (AA, Tuesday nights in St. Brendan's parish hall). Gloria hired him in 1995 when nobody would. Nights on
the line. A rosary tattooed around his left wrist (done inside); Vin Scully on a transistor; Louis L'Amour paperbacks in
his apron; the 9:30 at St. Brendan's, back row (he nods to Ray Ochoa; they don't talk). Gentle, slow, deadpan about the
dish machine ("Hobart. Older than you, meaner than Gloria"). Never volunteers prison, never hides it. His mother rode three
buses from Oxnard every other Sunday for six years. 🔒 **Role:** Jack's mirror for Danny; after E03 Jack can't look at
him for a while. E15: "I know what an errand is, mijo" (Rafi's offer). S8: Danny visits for graduation and he and Rudy talk
at the counter for an hour about nothing. Not a saint: a temper he manages like a stove.
**Voice.** Soft, unhurried, Westside Chicano; "mijo"; never raises it.
- *"First time I washed dishes was at Soledad. Six years. Best job in the building — it's warm, and nobody bothers the dish man."*

### 10.13 GIDEON STRAND — Lena's magnetic guy 🔒 (added for the player's directive)
*(Brief: "Lena's temptation: room builds him." He mirrors Sol: real pull, flaws where Julian's strengths are, indifferent
to her faith. Replaces Brooks Callahan as Lena's S2 rebound.)*
**Full name:** Gideon Asher Strand. Born **March 1, 1986** — 23 when he arrives (S2). Hyde Park, Chicago. Son of **Nathan
Strand**, a civil-rights litigator who has argued before the Supreme Court twice, and **Dr. Ruth Asher**, a political
theorist at the University of Chicago; secular Jewish, nothing in particular. Berkeley '08 (History); now a first-year
History MA at Marisol writing on the 1934 West Coast waterfront strike, and the lead organizer of the (fictional)
hotel-workers' campaign at the Marisol Harbor Inn — the housekeepers Lena has been bringing soup to since October.

**Who he is.** Brilliant, beautiful, reckless; the best arguer Lena has ever met who isn't Jack. Marx and Arendt and
Dorothy Day's own *The Long Loneliness*, which he quotes at her to win. Arrested twice at sit-ins and proud of it. He
**fights her** — on everything, as an equal, and she loves it (Julian, later, will never once). His flaws sit exactly where
Julian's strengths are: not gentle, not deferential, careless with people in small ways (forgets, uses friends for the
cause, is late to her birthday because of a picket), and **indifferent to her faith** — not hostile: "I'm not against God,
Counselor. I'm against waiting for Him." He sleeps around without apology and tells her so; he wants her plainly and says
that too. He calls her "Counselor."

**What he is to her.** Her politics without her faith: the Catholic Worker's left lean, set on fire and emptied of God.
He arrives in the raw weeks after Wes, when her resolve is lowest, and he is the most dangerous thing in her life because
he's right about half of it. **Hard rule:** they never sleep together. The near-miss (S2E10, his apartment over the union
hall, the night of the first strike vote) is where she stops, and his "Then what are we doing, Counselor?" is the end of
it (S2E12). Jack sees only what POV allows — Gideon's Saab idling outside Delgado at 2 a.m., Lena coming in late in his
coat, a door closing — and the fear is Jack's, never the narration's (§4.4); in S4 she tells him: never, not with anyone.

**The friends.** Theo openly hates him and is magnificently rude ("the Che poster with abs"); Jack hates him too and
**quietly thanks Theo for it** (one line, S2E09). Laney is perfectly polite to Gideon in the open and privately feeds
Theo his schedule. Margot thinks he's a fraud with good politics. Kevin asks everyone to be nice.

**Look.** Tall, rangy, curly dark hair he doesn't cut, a three-day beard, a gray union T-shirt under a good wool coat his
mother bought him, reading glasses he's vain about not wearing. A 1991 Saab 900 with a bumper sticker for a strike that
ended before he was born.

**Voice.** Fast, Chicago-flat, Socratic, delighted by his own cleverness and by hers; never says sorry, says "Fair."
- *"Your saint ran a soup kitchen for fifty years and the soup never once organized a union. Fair?"*
- *"I'm not asking you to stop believing. I'm asking you to stop waiting."*

**Arc.** **S2E02** (Jan 2010) at the Harbor Inn picket where Lena brings coffee; **S2E04** together; **proxy fight #1** with
Jack (S2E08, §24.6); the near-miss (S2E10); **S2E12** the end — she ends it, at the line. Returns SU3/S7 as a name on a
2012 campaign masthead; Lena reads it and closes the laptop.

## 11. THE NEWMAN CENTER CAST (St. Brendan the Navigator)

- **Fr. Gabriel Osei** (above).
- **Mrs. Dolores Fitch, 71.** Parish secretary since 1979. Widow. Lemon bars. Remembers every student by name and every
  sin by reputation. A gossip with a heart of gold leaf. Knew Nora; keeps her choir folder. *"You'll sing? We lost our
  best soprano last spring."* Source.
- **Veritas Missionaries** (a fictional national campus-ministry corps, two-year stints):
  - **Tyler Brandt, 26.** Cincinnati (St. Xavier HS — Jack's Ohio rival, Bengals vs. Browns, Moeller vs. Mooney in his
    head), former Miami (Ohio) linebacker. Relentless "discipleship" (wants to meet you for coffee *every week*). Genuinely
    holy and genuinely exhausting. Fist-bumps after Mass. *"Brother. Brother. Let's grab a coffee and talk about your
    prayer life."* Arc: in S4 he's the one who sits with Theo in county lockup for six hours.
  - **Bridget McNulty, 24.** Franciscan University of Steubenville '07. Soft-spoken, the sharpest theological mind in the
    building, secretly in a dark night of the soul she tells no one about. Lena's ally. S2: she leaves the missionaries
    mid-year; Lena is furious, then understands.
- **Earnest kids:**
  - **Joey Abruzzo**, freshman, Staten Island, b. Jan 17, 1991. Loud, joyful, wants to be a priest, says so constantly,
    is extremely interested in girls, says *that* constantly. Comic relief and a real vocation; he enters seminary in
    2013. Lives in Cabrillo Hall. *"Jack! JACK. You're Italian? Half? Half counts, the good half."*
  - **Becca Lindell** (above).
- **The rival couple — Peter Haddad and Kenzie Rourke.** Juniors. Peter (b. Mar 8, 1989): Orange County Maronite family,
  political science, Newman president, perfect, ambitious (wants to be a congressman), lector. Kenzie (b. Oct 1, 1989):
  Sacramento, nursing, cantor with a gorgeous voice, kind. Newman's "it couple"; they give the Theology of the Body talk
  every fall ("Love Is Patient: Our Chastity Story"). 🔒 **They are not chaste and haven't been since the summer of 2008.**
  Peter is terrified of being found out; Kenzie is tired of lying. Kenzie gets pregnant in S3; Peter's first instinct is
  the clinic, and Kenzie's is the opposite, and the Newman community gets a real-life test of everything it says. They
  marry (S3), divorce (adult seasons). They are the dark mirror of Jack and Lena: a couple whose performance of holiness
  is the problem, not their sin. Not villains: Peter's hypocrisy is fear; his eventual honesty is brave.
  **(E06 plan — the talk, Fri Sep 18, 7:30 p.m., Newman House lounge:)** a PowerPoint on a borrowed projector; 1 Cor 13:4
  on the title slide; photos (the Disneyland one; Kenzie's cousin's wedding); their slideshow song is Jason Mraz & Colbie
  Caillat, "Lucky"; Tyler MCs. They met at a Steubenville youth conference (summer 2007), "chose to wait" (🔒). At the Q&A
  **Margot reads Eph 5:22–24 (RSV) and asks Kenzie whether she plans to submit.** **Kenzie** answers with v. 21 and v. 25 —
  she'll submit to a man exactly as far as he's already laying his life down for her, and she'll know because he'll have been
  doing it every day before she's asked — better than Margot expected; Margot says so. **Peter**: "Practically? When we can't
  agree, I make the call. Prayerfully." Kenzie's face. If Jack asks *"What do you do when you fall?"*, Peter: **"By God's
  grace, we haven't had to find out."** Kenzie looks at her hands (🔒 the lie; she is tired of it). At the BBQ Kenzie tells
  Jack, quietly, "Good question. Nobody asks that one." Peter avoids him.
- **The choir:** led by **Gus Ferreira**, 60s, retired Portuguese-American tuna-fleet captain, organist, a voice like a
  foghorn.
- **The 9 p.m. Sunday Mass** is the college Mass: candles, guitar, standing room. The 11:30 is the "families and
  serious people" Mass; the pilot's collision happens there.

## 12. OTHER RECURRING PLAYERS

- **Doug Pruitt** (b. Aug 3, 1991), Kevin's roommate, Bakersfield, mechanical engineering, World of Warcraft (a level-80
  Tauren druid), sweet, oblivious, says "dude" with five vowels. 🔒 He is the 3 a.m. fire-alarm puller — a sleepwalker.
  Revealed S1E20.
- **Sloane Kittredge** (b. Dec 2, 1987), senior, Pi Lambda Chi rush chair, Laney's future "big," blonde, glossy, cool.
  🔒 Nora's sophomore roommate (Serra Hall 3B). She was at the Lighthouse on April 25 and saw Nora leave the first time.
  On the pilot night she leaves the cove bonfire with Theo (if Jack is there to see it) — neither of them knows what the
  other has to do with that night.
- **Det. Sgt. Ray Ochoa**, 49, Marisol PD, Rafi's father. Decent, tired, Catholic (St. Brendan's 9:30, back row). Closed
  the Calloway case as accidental — partly out of evidence, partly out of mercy to a family he privately believed had lost
  a daughter to suicide. 🔒 He knew about the pregnancy from the autopsy and kept it out of the public record at the
  Calloways' request. **(E06 plan — on-page debut:)** a gray unmarked Crown Victoria with a dash light; driving home at 1:30
  a.m. on Ocean Road, he recognizes his son's skateboard towing a kid on a bike with no light, lights them up, and does the
  bit: **"License and registration."** Dry, Westside, tired, funny in a dad's register; Spanish with Rafi; knows Jack as
  "Gloria's dish man — Rudy's kitchen" (he and Rudy nod at the 9:30 and don't talk). Asks his son what's in the bag; Rafi:
  "Vitamins, Pop"; **Ray does not open it** (🔒 the same mercy that closed the Calloway case — he chooses not to look; never
  juxtaposed with anything Nora). Gives Jack a clip-on bike light out of his trunk ("found property") and a line about
  Ocean Road: "I've picked enough kids up off this road." Jack learns: Rafi's father is a Marisol PD detective named Ray.
  **Lena knows the name Ochoa** (🔒 he took her statement April 26): in the E06 two-hander, when Jack says it, she touches the
  medal and changes the subject. One touch; no gloss; never again in S1 before E13.
- **Brooks Callahan** (b. July 4, 1988), senior, Sigma Rho, Newport Beach, lacrosse, Catholic-on-paper (Mater Dei).
  Charming, entitled, not evil; wants what he wants. **(Revised:)** no longer Lena's S2 rebound (that is Gideon, §10.13).
  He is Theo's Sigma Rho big when Theo rushes (S2) and Laney's date to the winter formal (Feb 2010), which ends with Laney
  leaving early in Theo's car. One date with Lena at most, early S2 — she leaves at dessert; Theo is rude to him anyway.
- **Dale Wisniewski**, 58 (added E05 plan). Warren, *Michigan* ("not the other one"); 27 years at Chrysler's Warren Truck
  Assembly (Dodge Rams), laid off in the spring of the Chrysler bankruptcy; moved into his daughter's spare room in Marisol
  in July. Barry Sanders jersey, a Lions fan through 0–16, sits alone at the end of Sully's bar on Sundays. Lena adopts him
  at the opener (the Lions are the Aints of the North). May recur on Saints Sundays as Lena's charity case and Jack's mirror
  for Frank. Never a sad-sack: dry, gentle, tips in quarters.
- **Gloria Mendes**, 63, night waitress at the Anchor diner for 30 years. Rates customers like pies (Jack: "Lattice-top.
  Pretty on top. Everything's leaking underneath."). Hires Jack as night dishwasher (S1E02: "Four out of ten? Hon, you're
  hired."): Tue/Thu/Sat, 7 p.m.–1 a.m., $8.00/hr (California minimum, 2009) plus a share of tips.
- **Tom and Rosa (Gutiérrez) Calloway**, Nora's parents, raisin farmers near Selma (Fresno County). 🔒 Their parish in
  Selma whispered suicide. Rosa has not been to Communion since — she believes she failed her daughter. Appear S4–S5.
- **Carly Benedetto**, 19, Youngstown, Jack's friend from Mooney, at Youngstown State. 🔒 The girl Jack hit Mikey
  Kostelnik for (§16, revised: Mikey was her ex, drunk, hitting her and dragging her toward his truck; no sexual
  element). She knows the truth. Appears in Youngstown episodes.
- **Michael "Mikey" Kostelnik**, 20, Struthers, Ohio. 🔒 The young man whose skull hit the cinderblocks. Traumatic brain
  injury: seizures, slowed speech, can't drive or work, lives with his mother Donna. Jack sends anonymous postal money
  orders to Donna Kostelnik every month from his Anchor wages.

# PART III — THE HIDDEN ARCHITECTURE

## 13. Hidden prior knowledge at the pilot (Knowledge Auditor: read this)

At 6:48 a.m. on August 29, 2009, before the first line of the pilot, these people already know things the player doesn't:

- **Lena** knows: Nora Calloway (her Preview Weekend host, April 24–26, 2009); the Lighthouse house and the path to the
  Point (Nora walked her past it on Friday night, April 24); Nora was pregnant (the test in the trash); that she herself
  removed and discarded the test; that she told police she slept through; that around 12:45 a.m. she heard a boy crying
  in the Serra Hall corridor and a door shut, and didn't look. Fr. Osei (she confessed to him April 26). Mrs. Fitch (met
  at the Preview brunch). Hale (postcard, 2008). She does **not** know Theo, Kevin, Evan, Julian (she knows *of* Julian
  — Nora mentioned "my friend Jules who writes songs"), or Jack.
- **Theo** knows: what he did; Kevin (the beach stairs, the floor of Kevin's host's room); Fr. Osei (his confession, July
  2009); the Lighthouse; the Point; Nora's name (from the news). He has never seen Lena (she was asleep in Serra; he never
  saw her). He has Nora's jacket in a duffel in his closet.
- **Kevin** knows: Theo, and what Theo looked like at 12:45 a.m. He does not know what happened. He knows Nora's name
  from the news and the Newman memorial Mass. He has seen Lena once (the Preview Weekend Newman brunch, across the room)
  and might recognize her. He knows Fr. Osei (Preview brunch; a summer email).
- **Fr. Osei** knows: Lena (her confession); Theo (his confession); Kevin (brunch); Nora (his parishioner and soprano).
  He knows, from the two confessions, more than anyone. He can use none of it, and he will not even let it change how he
  speaks to them in a way that would betray the seal.
- **Hale** knows: Jack (his nine-page letter, 2008); Lena (her twelve-page letter, 2008 — longer, he notes); Nora (his
  student; the forty-one seconds). He has never seen Jack or Lena's faces. He knows their names are on his PHIL 9H roster.
- **Evan** knows: Nora; the pregnancy; the text; the blond boy (not his name or face clearly).
- **Priya** knows: Nora (freshman roommate); the box.
- **Rafi** knows: he sold Nora two bars that night.
- **Julian** knows: Nora; the wall at the Point where they sang.
- **Mrs. Fitch** knows: Nora; Lena (Preview brunch: "the New Orleans girl who asked for the mantilla basket").
- **Sloane** knows: Nora; that Nora was "seeing someone older"; that Nora left the party around 10:50 p.m. upset.
- **Margot, Laney, Jack**: know none of these people and nothing about Nora.

## 15. The elision rule (how close third keeps Jack's secret honestly)

Jack is the POV and the player *is* Jack, so the narration must never lie about what Jack did. It may **elide**: it can
decline to narrate a memory. Jack thinks about Danny constantly; the narration says so. It shows Jack flinching from the
scar story, smoking when Danny's name comes up, sending money orders to a "D. Kostelnik" (S1E05, a detail with no
explanation), writing letters he doesn't send. It never says "Danny did it" in Jack's voice; it never says "Jack did it"
until the reveal. When the truth comes out (S2E21), every earlier scene must read as honest in retrospect: the player
should feel *Jack was carrying this the whole time and I was carrying it with him without knowing.*
- Allowed: "Jack doesn't think about that night. He's good at it."
- Not allowed: "Jack has never hurt anyone."

## 16. The eyebrow scar — canon of the tellings

Jack tells the story differently every time. Track every telling in `continuity.md`. Never repeat a version with the same
person. The **truth** (🔒; revised for the brief's hard rule — no sexual element): November 23, 2007, a bonfire in the lot
behind the dead Republic Steel works in Struthers; Mikey Kostelnik, 18, drunk, Carly Benedetto's ex-boyfriend of two
weeks, had already backhanded her once in front of everyone and had her by the wrist and the hair, dragging her toward
his pickup to drive her home himself, drunk, while she screamed that she wasn't going (🔒 the drunk driver Jack stopped —
a rhyme with Laney's Kaylee and Hale's two drivers, never stated); Jack, 16, pulled him off; Mikey swung first, wearing his
Struthers High class ring, and split Jack's left eyebrow; Jack hit him once, with everything; Mikey went down backward and
his head struck the cinderblock fire ring. Danny, 20, arrived at the run, took one look, took Jack's bloody hand and wiped
it on his own shirt, and when the Struthers police came said, "It was me."
**Tellings used on the played path (after S1E02):** Theo — swan; Lena — pierogi, Archbishop; Osei — "a fight, a long
time ago" (honest, partial); the floor — none. **E03** offers Margot her first (she asks "for the file"); the Pacific one is
saved for whoever asks in the water.
**After S1E03:** Margot — Sister Mary Clement, third grade, a ruler (`scar_told_margot=="nun"`; "Predictable," underlined
twice; it is on her whiteboard file from E04). Nobody asked in the water. **E04** offers no telling (a grief week; nobody
asks). Still unused with Lena: the hockey puck, the husband, the swan, the Franciscan, the Pacific, Gina's controller.
**After S1E04:** no telling (as planned). **E05** offers **Kevin** his first (fork B, the Brick, when his ER story sends
Jack's hand to the eyebrow): bank options for him — the hockey puck, the Franciscan, the Pacific, or the honest partial "a
fight, a long time ago" (Osei's version; Kevin is the second person to get it). Never the girl.
**After S1E05:** no telling (pref fork; Kevin was never asked). **E06** offers **Becca** her first, at the waterline during
the cleanup — **"I walked into the Pacific Ocean"** is finally the one for whoever asks in the water; also the Franciscan
(charm), Gina's controller, or the honest partial "a fight, a long time ago" (Becca would be the second to get it, after
Osei). Kevin's first remains banked.
**After S1E06:** Becca → **the Franciscan** (`scar_told_becca=="franciscan"`; Eli and Father Anselm — "It's always the monks").
The Pacific is still banked for whoever asks in the water. **E07** offers no telling (nobody asks; the misread week is not
Kevin's week). Still unused with Lena: the hockey puck, the husband, the swan, the Franciscan, the Pacific, Gina's controller.
**After S1E07:** no telling (as planned). **E08** offers none on the page: on the moonlit cove Lena may *look* at the eyebrow and
not ask (one clause, if anywhere) — the next telling she gets should be later and should cost more. Kevin's first remains banked
(S1E16 is his week); "A girl. That one's true." stays reserved for Lena, much later.
🔒 **The hand (for the elision):** Jack is right-handed; the punch was his right. When Mark 9:43 is read (Sun Sep 27, 2009 —
"If your hand causes you to sin, cut it off"), the narration may let his right hand do one small thing and say nothing about
why.
**Tellings bank** (each usable once per listener): a hockey puck at a Youngstown Phantoms game; a nun with a ruler (Sr.
Mary Clement, 3rd grade, "she was aiming for my soul"); a jealous husband in Erie, Pennsylvania; a swan in Mill Creek Park;
knife-juggling at the St. Anthony's pierogi festival (the Ukrainians did it); a Franciscan who "plays dirty in pickup";
"I walked into the Pacific Ocean"; "My sister, with a Nintendo 64 controller" (Gina will confirm it, falsely, if asked —
she covers for him without knowing why); a cat named Archbishop; "A girl. That one's true." (He says that last one only
to Lena, much later, and it is — Carly — and it isn't.)

## 17. JACK'S SECRET (full)

**What happened:** §16. Mikey Kostelnik lived. He has a traumatic brain injury. Struthers PD found Danny with blood on his
shirt and Jack's scholarship-track future on the bench in the back of the cruiser. Danny had a prior (a 2006 bar fight,
misdemeanor assault) and was on probation; the Mahoning County prosecutor charged felonious assault; Danny took a plea
for five years in January 2008. At the station at 4 a.m., Jack, sixteen, tried once to tell a detective the truth; Frank
walked in and took him out of the room by the back of his neck. On the bench in the hall, Jack prayed for the first time
in his life like someone was there. He has been praying like that ever since. **His faith was born the night he became a
coward.** That's the knot.

**Why it matters to the show:** Jack spends seasons judging Theo's silence — and Lena's — while living inside his own. The
central mystery is a mirror: everyone who loves Theo will be asked what Jack's family already chose. Hale's specialty is
*moral luck* (Williams, Nagel): the idea that we are judged for outcomes we didn't control. Jack threw one punch in
defense of a girl; Mikey hit a cinderblock. Theo grabbed a girl to save her; she fell. Hale's seminar will put the
question to Jack in the abstract in S1E05 and he will argue it like his life depends on it, and the player won't know
why yet.

**The money orders.** Jack sends $20–$60 monthly to Donna Kostelnik, unsigned. Shown without explanation from S1E05.

**The clock.** 🔒 Jack is aware, vaguely and with dread, that there are time limits on prosecutions, and that if he does
nothing long enough, nothing can be done. *Writers: never specify the law on the page. The dread is moral, not legal.*
The show's position: he will tell, and the telling (S7, Fall 2012, to Mikey's mother first, then to the Mahoning County
prosecutor) costs him; what the law does about it is less important than what Donna Kostelnik does — 🔒 she slams the
door, and then, in S8, she comes to Danny's welcome-home Mass.

**Danny's release:** judicial release granted November 2011 (S5) — or the full term to January 2013, depending on whether
Jack has told the truth by then (S5 path flag). Danny at Jack's graduation, May 2013. Danny as godfather to Jack's first
child.

## 18. LENA'S SECRETS (full)

**A. The Preview Weekend (tied to the central mystery).**
- April 24, 2009 (Friday): Lena, 17, arrives for Marisol Preview Weekend. St. Brendan's runs a hosting program for Catholic
  admits; her host is **Nora Calloway** (Serra Hall 3B; Sloane was away for the night at the Pi Lam formal in Santa
  Barbara). Nora is luminous, funny, kind, a soprano; they stay up talking about Catherine of Siena and boys. Nora walks
  her past the Lighthouse to the Point at sunset on Friday and shows her the wall: "This is where I come to yell at God."
  Lena falls a little in love with the idea of becoming her.
- April 25 (Saturday): Nora is distracted all day; disappears for an hour after lunch (she bought the test at Walgreens,
  2:14 p.m.). Newman admit dinner 6–8 p.m. At 10:30 p.m. Nora goes to "a party for an hour"; asks Lena to come; Lena
  declines (she's seventeen, tired, and a good girl in a strange town). Nora returns at 11:15, shaking, goes into the
  bathroom; Lena, pretending to sleep, hears her crying and praying the Memorare. At 11:40 Nora kneels by Lena's air
  mattress and whispers: *"Cover for me, Magdalena. If anyone asks, I'm in the shower."* Leaves.
- 11:45 p.m.: Lena gets up for water and sees the test in the bathroom trash. Two lines.
- ~12:45 a.m.: Lena hears a boy crying in the corridor — a broken, gasping sound — then a door down the hall open and shut.
  She doesn't look. She's covering.
- April 26, 7:40 a.m.: the Serra RA knocks: there's been an accident. Before police arrive, Lena takes the test out of the
  trash, wraps it in a Newman bulletin, walks it to the dumpster behind Serra Hall, and comes back. To Det. Ochoa she says
  she was asleep by eleven and Nora was "fine. A little sad. Normal sad." She touches her grandmother's medal the whole
  time.
- April 26, 2:00 p.m.: she goes to St. Brendan's and makes her confession to Fr. Osei: she lied to the police and she
  threw away something that belonged to a dead girl. He absolves her and tells her, gently and firmly, that she should
  tell the police the truth. She doesn't. She tells herself she's protecting a dead girl's name and a family in Fresno. 🔒
  The irony she doesn't know: the autopsy already told the police about the pregnancy. Her sin hid nothing. Her guilt is
  real anyway.
- She turns down Notre Dame (partial aid) for Marisol (Regents' Scholarship, full tuition) and tells everyone it was the
  money. It was also Nora: "I owe her four years."
- She never told anyone about the boy crying in the corridor. She has thought about him every night.

**B. Grandmère and Memorial (family rupture).** See §10.9. Lena knows her grandmother died at Memorial during Katrina; she
knows her mother was working in the building and left on a boat on the fourth day; she has never asked her mother a single
question about it. She has read every public article about the Memorial deaths and hates herself for it. Celeste's text
on August 30, 2009 (*"Don't read the Times this week, cher."*) is the first time her mother has acknowledged that there is
anything to read.

**C. Wes.** She knows something's wrong. She has noticed he hasn't mentioned Mass since Labor Day. She's decided not to
notice.

**D. The postcard.** Hale wrote her the same line he wrote Jack. She came, in part, to say it to his face.

planned). Losses during college fall on family, guests and the wider cast (Wes; Nonna).
