## 22. SEASON 1 — FALL 2009 (22 episodes, Aug 29 – Dec 18, 2009)

*Liturgical year B through Christ the King (Nov 22); Year C from the First Sunday of Advent (Nov 29). Classes begin Mon
Aug 31; Labor Day Sept 7; Thanksgiving break Nov 25–29; finals Dec 14–18.*

**E01 · "Lip Service"** · Sat Aug 29 – Sun Aug 30 · 22nd Sunday OT (Mark 7). Ted Kennedy's funeral Mass in Boston; the
Station Fire above La Cañada; Frank sees the Pacific. Move-in, Theo, the floor meeting; the Lighthouse party; the back
porch; Mass at St. Brendan's; the first duel; Hale in a diner booth; the stairwell call ("Wes"); sand on Theo's feet.

**E02 · "The Examined Life"** · Mon Aug 31 – Fri Sep 4 · St. Gregory the Great (Thu Sept 3; weekday Gospel Luke 5:1–11,
"Depart from me, Lord, for I am a sinful man"); Ramadan; full moon Fri Sept 4. *(Re-planned after S1E01; full plan in
`outlines/s1e02.md`.)* Cold open: the first 3 a.m. fire alarm, the whole floor on the lawn; Theo meets Lena ("I like her.
She's mean"). Hale's PHIL 9H: "Prove to me you have a soul" — a four-move duel **against Hale**, Lena as rival and ally
(Ryle's ghost; 🔒 Phineas Gage; Lena vs. Jack on form vs. interior; the genetic debunking); Hale pairs Jack and Lena as
**designated antagonists** for the semester (weekly papers, annotated in each other's margins). New at the table: Caleb
Hargrove, Yusuf Noorzai. Margot meets Lena in PHIL 1 and says "useful idiots" aloud. Gloria hires Jack ("Four out of ten?
Hon, you're hired"); Rudy Salcedo; Rafi lends him La Flaca. Frank home ("How's the pelican?"); Traficant; the letter to
Danny (elided). Osei: "the box or the board" (fork). Daily Mass; Kevin's lunch (a smooth lie about Theo). Evan's Thursday
section — the driveway man. Lena names Wes to Jack's face; the second cigarette ("I'm going to need a container").
Facebook: In a Relationship; 🔒 Nora's candle; 🔒 Wes awake at 3:58 a.m. Laney's Pi Lam open house (fork: with Lena / with
Margot and Kevin). The full-moon beach; Lena teaches the Who Dat. Theo's 450SL and the pitch: "Full moon, Costigan. LA is
three hours. Get in the car." (`e2_la`). Becca invites Jack to the Sept 19 BBQ.

**E03 · "Labor"** · Sat Sep 5 – Mon Sep 7 (Labor Day) · 23rd Sunday OT (Is 35; **James 2:1-5**, the rich man with
the gold ring; Mark 7:31-37, *Ephphatha*). *(Re-planned after S1E02 for the played path — `e2_la=="broke"`; full plan in
`outlines/s1e03.md`.)* **Player note: Lena lighter and funnier — a breather after E02's ache, so the next ache (E04,
Nora's birthday) lands harder.** The episode is a comedy weekend with one knife, and the knife is Jack's, not Lena's.
Theo's nine a.m. ambush with a full tank turns into a **convoy** (the SL; Kevin's mother's Odyssey with Kevin, Margot,
Laney in the Mooney hoodie, and Lena, who "isn't going"); Jack can afford his share of gas (he tells them about the
Anchor — Lena's first she's heard of it). Fork: ride in the SL with Theo or the van with everyone. **Pea Soup Andersen's,
Buellton: the Reliquary tin** (an Altoids tin from the gift shop; the mints poured into Kevin's palm; Saturday's
cigarette sealed in; Sharpie on the lid). Malibu: Station Fire ash on the deck chairs ("Don't touch the deck chairs.
That's the mountains."); the fridge nobody may eat from; the Oscar in the powder room. Lena makes groceries and cooks
(a roux is a vow; Jack stirs while Wes calls on time and she laughs on the deck); **Jack pays Theo his pie** (peach,
Lena's mother's crust) and Theo asks whether God takes IOUs. Rock Band. Sunday: the 8 a.m. at Our Lady of Malibu — James 2
in a Malibu church — Lena coaching Laney through her first Mass in whispers; Theo outside with coffees ("a supporter, not
a member"); Nonna on the phone about First Friday. Fork: **the Getty Villa with Lena (and Margot, who out-Greeks her)**
or the pool with Theo, Laney and Kevin (Poppy on the first ring). **Richard Vance's six minutes** (Margot times him:
6:14): magnetic and awful; mistakes Jack at the sink for help and tips him a hundred. **The labor duel** on the deck that
night, Margot moderating: the just wage, the right of association (*Rerum Novarum*; *Caritas in Veritate* §25), the UAW,
the bailout, *Laborem Exercens* ("work is for man") — and Frank, laid off since June, said aloud to her for the first
time. **She wins it** (2–1). Monday, Labor Day, 7:12 a.m.: *"This is a prepaid call from — Danny — an inmate at Trumbull
Correctional Institution."* Theo in the kitchen; Danny funny and gentle and asking what the ocean looks like; Theo
handles it beautifully and keeps it. Then Lena on the beach stairs, the tin's first deposit, and a cold swim she dares
him into without knowing why he needed it. The drive home: Obama at the AFL-CIO picnic in Cincinnati on the radio;
Gina: dad's in the garage with the radio. Tag: Theo, in the dark: "What's he reading?"

**E04 · "Nativity"** · Tue Sep 8 – Sat Sep 12 · Nativity of Mary (Sept 8) — 🔒 Nora's birthday. *(Re-planned after S1E03
for the played path and the player's notes — the harder turn after the breather; ensemble setpieces as punctuation, not
every week; **a long Jack/Lena two-hander as the emotional centerpiece**. Full plan in `outlines/s1e04.md`.)* Row M empty
(PHIL 1, Hale on Epicurus); the 12:10 Nativity Mass (Osei on the genealogy: "God is not ashamed of his family"); "Happy
birthday to her." / "To who?"; Fitch's white rose. Jack's first shift beside Rudy since Danny's voice (the every-other-Sunday
rhyme lands: "Whoever you got — write him"); the Anchor's kitchen goes dark Thursday (annual hood cleaning: a lost shift, a
free night); **Hale drunk in the back booth, Housman, "Go home, Mr. Costigan"** (🔒). Priya's cupcake: Nora becomes a person.
Wednesday: Hale's clean jacket; Locke and Reid's brave officer; the paper swap ("(Costigan)"; his paper "That God Is the One
Who Pays"); **"You lie!"** in the 4th-floor lounge (a room scene, not a setpiece) — Margot's whiteboard debuts; Jack hears the
racial note for the first time (brief's long arc); Lena declines the fight ("I'm too tired to argue with a whiteboard");
Laney hands her to Jack for the week. Fork: the Point at sunset (the cross, the jasmine, **Julian**) or iftar with Yusuf
(Tariq in Helmand). Thursday: Evan's "PERSONAL"; **Café Bastille — "Fresno"; Lena leaves in the second chorus. THE
TWO-HANDER** (~4,400 words, five scenes, nobody else): the end of the harbor pier; No. 3 (she smokes half); Hopkins' "Thou
art indeed just, Lord" with *Show Lena* in his margin; **her grandmother, the storm, the medal in a plastic bag, the mother
she has never asked**; *Who are you paying for, Costigan?*; **Danny, if the player tells her**; Ocean Road in Danny's jacket
("Nobody yells at an empty room"); counting freight cars on the Delgado steps; "Thank you, Jack"; "Goodnight, Magdalena"
(gated). Friday 9/11: Theo drives Jack to Driftwood to ship the Waugh to Trumbull (the replacement Everyman, "Not on the tab";
Richard's four days in a Lincoln from Toronto, 2001; "I'm good at not asking"). Saturday: the box or the sprinkler (Nonna's
"Go next week"). Tag: Jack's first Saturday shift; olallieberry 9/10; Lena brings the jacket back; Gloria rates her "Pecan";
**Wes calls at 2:04 a.m. Central from a party** — "He sounded happy." Lena's resolve 8 → 5 (4 at the song). No duel.

**E05 · "Moral Luck"** · Sun Sep 13 – Fri Sep 18, 2:10 a.m. · 24th Sunday OT (Is 50; Jas 2:14-18; **Mark 8:27-35**, "Who
do you say that I am?" / "Get behind me, Satan" / "take up his cross"); VMAs (Kanye/Taylor, Sept 13); Exaltation of the Cross
(Mon Sept 14) and Patrick Swayze's death; Our Lady of Sorrows (Tue Sept 15) and the House rebukes Wilson; Baucus's draft
(Wed 16); *Community* premieres (Thu 17); Ramadan's last week. *(Re-planned after S1E04 for the played path and the notes —
no two-hander this week (E06 owes one); **an ensemble setpiece is due**: the Saints opener at Sully's. Full plan in
`outlines/s1e05.md`.)* **Sully's, 10 a.m., black and gold** (Jack owns neither: Margot's black Sleater-Kinney T-shirt or
Theo's closet; Lena on the door with gold beads), the whole core in a booth, Dale the laid-off Lions man from Warren,
*Michigan*, the Browns dying on the corner TV (Favre's Vikings), **Brees's six touchdowns**, Lena painting a gold fleur-de-lis
on Jack's cheekbone at halftime, "Who dat, Costigan. Say it like you mean it," Laney's "Bid Day, sugar. Wear something you
can cry in," Wes texting from the Dome. Sunday 9 p.m. Mass: Osei on Peter, right and wrong in one afternoon; Nonna has
called the rectory to pay for a Mass "in thanksgiving" (Osei can neither confirm nor deny a thing). **Monday: the
moral-luck duel** (Hale's two drivers; Lena the opponent; Hale's "Forgiven by whom?"; Yusuf's diya; "Do you think the
lucky one sleeps?"); the cloister ("You argued that like it was yours" — "It's about me"); **Gina's text: "the convict's
sister"** (shown to Lena or not) and the stairwell call; 3 a.m. *Point Break* with Theo for Swayze. Tuesday: **the money
order to D. Kostelnik, Struthers, Ohio — twenty dollars, the minimum, out of tips; no explanation.** Wednesday: Gauguin;
the paired papers ("That the Lucky Man Owes Nothing" — the thing he least believes); **fork:** 217 after Laney's Pref Night
(Lupe's carnitas, the DRP pillbox, Laney's terror of being chosen) or the Brick with Kevin (the ER, "not a scratch," a scar
telling). Thursday 1 a.m.: **the jacket** ("A girl left it"). Thursday: Evan back from "PERSONAL" (the third driver);
**Pi Lam Bid Day** on Seacliff (Laney runs home in a Pi Lam Rodeo shirt; Margot's *Tide* piece is on the stands); on the
bluff bench, **a prepaid call from Trumbull with Lena beside him** — "Hi, Danny" (path) — cut off by the system mid-sentence:
"Tell Gina — ." Tag: the 2:10 freight on the Delgado steps; Lena texts a number. Lena's resolve 5 → 7.

**E06 · "Welcome"** · Fri Sep 18 – Sun Sep 20 (tag Sun ~11:50 p.m.) · 25th Sunday OT Year B (Wis 2:12, 17-20; Ps 54;
**Jas 3:16–4:3**, "you covet but do not possess"; **Mark 9:30-37**: the argument on the road about who is greatest, silence in
the house, "If anyone wishes to be first, he shall be the last of all and the servant of all," the child — "whoever receives one
such child in my name, receives me"); Rosh Hashanah from sundown Friday; Eid al-Fitr Sunday; Ohio State 38–0 Toledo in
Cleveland (Sat); **Saints W 48–22 at Philadelphia** (Sun, 10 a.m. PT; the glass goes 2–0); Browns L 6–27 at Denver. *(Re-planned
after S1E05 for the played path and the notes — full plan in `outlines/s1e06.md`. **The standing-rule extended two-hander is
owed and is the episode's heart:** "Walk me home, Costigan. The long way." Player loved Lena, Theo and Laney; Margot and Hale;
Danny and Gina — keep investing in Jack's family. No duel; no setpiece: the chastity talk, the cleanup and the BBQ are mid-size
rooms.)* **Cold open:** fire alarm 2, Fri 3:12 a.m., fifty-eight minutes after "Goodnight, Jack"; Laney in the pink Rodeo hat
("They were *lucky*, fireman"); Margot opens an alarm log ("Nineteen days. That's not a schedule. That's a person."); Doug
barefoot at the wrong door again (no comment); **Theo carries out both bags off the top shelf and no espresso machine** ("It's
insured" — 🔒 one sentence); Lena, who has seen Becca's wall post: **"It's a beach cleanup, Jack. It is not a date. Bring
gloves."** **Friday:** Hale's office hours (Hollis 314, by email summons) — Hale lends Jack his own Oxford copy of Williams's
*Moral Luck* ("Bring it back with your margins in it"), asks how an eighteen-year-old knows the lucky one doesn't sleep; Jack
may tell him about Danny (gated; 🔒 the S3 footnote); "Miss Broussard was here at two." **Fork:** Theo's cut of the Sully's
footage in 412 ("Look at your face, Costigan") or Margot's last legal cloves on Mission ("I need a pallbearer"). Gina 4:12 p.m.:
"i said it. danny. she looked away first." **Friday night: "Love Is Patient: Our Chastity Story"** (Newman lounge; Peter &
Kenzie's slideshow, Jason Mraz; Joey; Becca's sincere question; Kevin's smile at "your future wife" and Margot watching Kevin —
🔒 E07); **Margot reads Eph 5:22–24 and asks Kenzie whether she plans to submit**; Kenzie's v. 21/v. 25 answer is better than
Margot expected; Peter's "I make the call. Prayerfully." isn't; Jack and Lena look at each other and table it ("Not tonight" —
§24.5); Jack may ask "What do you do when you fall?" → "By God's grace, we haven't had to find out" (🔒). **Saturday:** Becca's
beach cleanup at Campus Point (her team; pink spare gloves; seven-or-nine children; the scar at the waterline — the Pacific; a
Las Olas condom mistaken for a jellyfish and Becca's "I've pulled a calf"; Lena thirty yards down the beach with Joey, laughing;
Jack and Lena trade *Caritas in Veritate* §48–51 across the sand); the BBQ in the courtyard (Osei at the grill; the sprinkler at
grace; Tyler's coffee; Kenzie's "Good question"); **Becca: "I told my mother this was a date. Was it?"** → date #1, retroactively.
5 p.m.: **Danny's first letter on the page** ("Tell Gina it ain't hers to carry. I got it." 🔒; "Tell the veil girl thanks for
asking my name"); the call home (OSU 38–0 in Cleveland — Frank yelled at the TV "in a good way"); **Nonna asks "E la ragazza?"
twice.** The Anchor (Rudy: "You wrote him?" "He wrote back."). **Ocean Road, 1:31 a.m.: "License and registration."** — Ray
Ochoa's bit for his son; "Pop."; the bag he doesn't open (🔒); a bike light from his trunk; Rafi after: "Not bars, though."
**THE TWO-HANDER** (~4,300, Sun 1:52–3:55 a.m.; nobody else after the porch): the Pi Lam barn party (Laney carried upstairs by
her sisters — "Take her home, fireman, she's being a *mother*"; Theo staying to watch her, "I'm up anyway"); **"Walk me home,
Costigan. The long way."**; east, down the bluff stairs below the bench where she said hi to Danny; the dark beach (two nights
past new moon); the 2:10 freight on the bluff above them, which neither counts; **the creek mouth** (her hand on his forearm —
the one restraint sentence; 🔒); the Campus Point lifeguard tower — **Becca** ("because it seemed like the right thing to want"
→ "That's the worst reason I've ever heard, and I've heard you defend the Browns") and **Wes** (missed 8:04, called at 9:40 from
somewhere loud; "he calls from somewhere loud a lot"; **"I've decided not to notice"** — the partying only, 🔒 no Mass); "Don't.
Don't be funny right now. Just stay." (if he jokes); up the Marine Lab stairs and across the dark Commons; **Ochoa** (medal;
subject change — 🔒); **Danny's letter** ("It ain't hers to carry"); Mémère calls her by her aunt's name and she answers to it;
"Why Marisol?" "Money." (medal 🔒) / "You know why I came" (🔒 the card); the Lagoon bench where people go to break up —
**"We'd have to be something first, Costigan."** (end-card tag). **Sunday 6:10 a.m.:** Theo in with wet hems — **"I run now."**
**Fork:** Sully's with Lena and Dale (2–0; "Next week, kid" paid) or Eid with Yusuf (Tariq calls from Helmand). **Sunday 9 p.m.
Mass:** Osei — "On the road they argued about who was the greatest. In the house He asked them what they'd been talking about,
and they were silent"; Laney at her second Mass in her Pi Lam letters under Lena's cardigan. **Tag:** Jack writes Danny back.
Lena's resolve 7 → 6 (the party) → 5 (Wes named) → 7 (Mass). *(Supersedes the earlier E06 notes: the grandmother and the Danny
exchange were spent in E04–E05; Lena already knows Danny and has spoken to him.)*

**E07 · "Cloves"** · Tue Sep 22 – Sun Sep 27 (extended through Sunday so the Saints at Buffalo and the 26th Sunday land in
an episode; E08 opens Tue Sep 29) · FDA flavored-cigarette ban (Tue); the equinox (Tue 2:18 p.m. PDT); Padre Pio (Wed); the
G-20 in Pittsburgh (Thu–Fri; Qom announced Fri); **Ohio State 30–0 Illinois** (Sat); **Saints 27–7 at Buffalo (3–0)**; 26th
Sunday OT B (Num 11:25–29; Jas 5:1–6; **Mark 9:38–48** — "if your hand causes you to sin"). *(Re-planned after S1E06 for the
played path and the notes — full plan in `outlines/s1e07.md`. **Player: lean in on the precipice** — the realization a little
closer, nothing crossed, Wes still there, slow burn; no two-hander owed (E08); Lena central; Becca keeps her blend.)* Cold open:
**Margot's funeral card** under 412's door (this path's first invitation: "Bring pie. The good pie, Costigan."). **Nonna's
Mass** at the 12:10 (Luke 8:19–21, "my mother and my brothers"), Lena beside him; Becca: "Friday. Six. I'll drive." **Gina
friends Lena on Facebook** — and finds the relationship status, and Becca's wall post. The Anchor: Ray at the counter; Gloria's
pie for a funeral. **The wake on the Delgado roof** (ALARM WILL SOUND — *it won't*): **"Dearly beloved. We are gathered here
today to bury a cigarette."**; the eulogy's "my own body" turns into **the 2 a.m. argument** (Margot vs. Lena, Kevin and Jack
at the table) — **"This stopped being a game an hour ago."** — **Anders**; neither concedes; both cry; Lena asks his name. Kevin
walks Margot down (🔒 he comes out to her; Jack misreads them as a couple). **Jack and Lena alone on the roof till sunrise**
(~1,800 words; not a full two-hander): "You argued that like it was yours"; the sentence she didn't finish at the tower — she
still won't; **Wes has bought a ticket for Columbus Day**. Margot's whiteboard: TUESDAY — NO SCORE. (BEREAVEMENT.)
**Wednesday, no sleep: the friendship duel** in Hale's seminar — their papers, "That a Man and a Woman Can Be Friends" (Jack)
and "That They Can't" (Lena); Lena favored; Hale: one of them has proved the other's thesis. Hale's Housman for Danny (gated).
Tyler's coffee. Thursday: Kevin or Margot (the misread); section (Evan, Kierkegaard); Lena keeps his paper: "Sunday."
**Friday: Becca date #2** — the Fishbowl (Laney: "Is she Amish or just punctual?"; Lena: "Laney. Be nice."; Theo flawless,
texting; Kevin: "Leave it alone.") — Marisol High football in her brother's Ranger; the First Way on the bleachers; a kiss on
the cheek "so there's no confusion." Saturday: the call home ("Who's Idaho?" — "And the veil girl?"). Sunday fork: Sully's with
Lena and Dale (3–0) or the 9:30 and Coffee & Donuts with Becca; Osei on the hand. **Tag: her red ink, under the door —
"Defense."** Lena's resolve 7 → 8 → 6 → **5** → 6. No danger window. Duel record 3–1 → 3–2 on the default (Lena) result.

**E08 · "The Little Way"** · Tue Sep 29 – Sun Oct 4 · Michaelmas (Tue; Jack's confirmation saint); St. Jerome (Wed); **St.
Thérèse (Thu Oct 1; Lena's middle name)**; Guardian Angels (Fri); the Blessing of the Animals (Sat Oct 3, a day before Francis);
27th Sunday B (Gen 2:18 "It is not good for the man to be alone"; Mark 10:2–16); the Samoa tsunami and the Padang quake on the
Fishbowl TV; Chicago loses the Olympics (Fri); OSU 33–14 at Indiana; **Saints 24–10 Jets (4–0)**; Browns lose in OT (0–4).
*(Re-planned after S1E07 — retitled from "Michaelmas"; full plan in `outlines/s1e08.md`. **The two-hander is the player's own
design** (brief, "S1E08 two-hander") and the episode is built around it.)* Since "Defense." they have been **scrupulously just
friends** — Monday's seminar ("Good afternoon, Jack"), Tuesday's Michaelmas 12:10 ("Happy feast day, Jack"; "We're fine, Jack"),
Wednesday's seminar on lying, where they agree with each other all hour and Hale is appalled. Tuesday: **Danny's Michaelmas
letter** ("Happy St. Mike's, Professor… Tell Lena hey… Don't feel bad about it… That's an order."); Theo: **"Does he catch
anybody? Or just throw?"** (🔒). Wednesday night, **THE TWO-HANDER** (~4,500; the Romance chunk): the Delgado laundry room at
ten — Jack erasing the Hopkins for Trumbull; Lena with two baskets; awkward, painful, polite ("How's Becca?" "Great." "How's
Wes?" "Great." "Nine days."); **she erases *Show Lena* herself** ("Friends help."). Midnight is her feast: he gives her a used
*Story of a Soul* with his pencil margins in it — **"You wrote in it. You *wrote in it*, Costigan."** — he reaches for the eraser;
"Don't you dare." **The crack** (a third of the way into the night): an unofficial duel on the Little Way, no referee ("Easy for
you." / the table of sinners / *empty hands*); Lupe's at one ("Para los novios." "We're friends." "Claro."); **Shipwreck Cove under
a gibbous moon going down into the Pacific** — Thérèse read aloud by moonlight, a Cajun two-step on the hard sand (or not),
**"Do you know what you're doing yet?"** — he starts, can't, admits he doesn't know, and says Becca is so nice, so sweet; **Lena
laughs before she can stop it** ("Come up with a better answer before somebody else asks you, Youngstown. That is the worst thing
you can say about a person in this situation.") **and can't hide her relief; he sees it.** At two, Becca is praying for him (her
promise). They walk back guilty; at the Delgado elevator, ~4 a.m.: **"That was a terrible thing to be glad about."** Laney, 4:41:
she came in and went straight to her knees, "not asking. (asking)"; Theo: "Did it come clean?" **From the guilt:** Thursday Jack
ships the Hopkins and Hale's Housman, stands behind Lena at the UCen counter while she argues a jar of the Pacific into the mail
for Wes, and from the east stairwell **asks Becca out for Friday — he plans it, he pays** (Friday sauce / elephant seals /
horses); Gloria rates Idaho sight unseen ("Apple. That's the one you bring home to your mother.") and asks after the pecan.
Friday, **date #3: Becca thrilled** ("Nobody's ever planned a thing for me"; "I've been praying for two in the morning"). **Lena
turns to Wes** (calls she makes; the stairwell laugh; the jar; Wes thrilled). Saturday, **the Blessing of the Animals** (the
ensemble setpiece): Thomas the crab ("Who brings a *crab*."); Hale and Bishop Berkeley ("He's deaf, Father. You'll have to bless
him louder."); Osei's hand on the dog's ribs; Berkeley and Knox's limerick; **neither resolve takes — the freeze lasts about five
minutes** and Jack and Lena are laughing together in front of Becca; Becca's question makes Osei laugh for a full minute; Mrs.
Fitch's "you two"; Margot's trial faith column; Jack's last Kevin-and-Margot joke, shut down, and he stops. Call home ("E
l'altra?"); optional confession (Lena coming out of the coat closet as he goes in). Sunday fork: Sully's (Sharper's 99 yards; the
bell; Wes from the Dome, "TELL THE OHIO GUY WHO DAT") or the 9:30 with Becca (Lena's play-by-play texts under the table). Osei on
Genesis 2: the first thing in the Bible that is not good is loneliness. Tag: the full moon in the six-inch window, Lena's laugh
on the stairs ("Five days"), Becca's "Friday was the best night." Lena's resolve 6 → 7 → 6 → 5 → **4** (the cove) → 5 → 7
(confession, Sat) → **6**. No danger window (Lena never drops below 4; both must be ≤3).

**E09 · "Columbus"** · Fri Oct 9 – Mon Oct 12 · Obama wins the Nobel Peace Prize (Oct 9); Columbus Day. **Wes visits.**
Jack expects to hate him and doesn't. Saints are 4–0. The beach at 2 a.m., the Jameson, the attic story — **told as
healed** ("Then she came along and I could") — and **the ring** on his phone (Mémère Odile's; he'll ask Tony after she
graduates). "Take care of
her." (🔒 Timing tragedy: Jack walks off the beach believing it's forever and stops waiting.) Lena sees Jack and Wes come back at dawn arm in arm and doesn't know what to do with her face. **(After E08:)** Wes arrives having opened the jar (he brings it back, empty, as a joke — "I drank it"); Lena is attentive to him in a way Jack recognizes as the week's resolve; Jack, guilty, is generous to Wes, and that generosity is what makes Wes trust him on the beach. The retreat-that-never-takes (#66) pauses for the weekend: with Wes in the room there is nothing to retreat from.

**E10 · "Midterms"** · Tue Oct 13 – Sat Oct 17 · "Balloon Boy" (Oct 15). The library all-nighter: Jack and Lena, the
eighth floor of Pacifica Library, 4 a.m., restraint-is-the-fire scene #1 (her head on his shoulder; he doesn't move for an
hour; his arm dies; he doesn't move). Priya's breakdown over orgo: she shows Jack Nora's box in the RA
storage closet and won't open the journal (moved from E11). Laney's Xanax and a pledge event; Lena and Laney's first real fight; Laney tells
Jack about Kaylee, drunk, on the Delgado steps. **Becca's first crack:** "You look at me like I'm homework." — and she
doesn't end it: "Then do your homework, Jack. I'll wait." (The tryer tries.) **(After E08:)** the crack lands harder after date #3: he planned and paid for a beautiful night out of guilt, and she felt the effort and, under it, the somewhere-else (E08: "You're always somewhere else a little").

**E11 · "Big Sur"** · Tue Oct 20 – Sun Oct 25 · The Vatican announces the personal ordinariates for Anglicans (Oct 20).
**The road-trip episode** (brief, Season 1 wish; a bottle episode on the move). Theo's 450SL holds two, so Kevin borrows his
mother's Odyssey: Jack, Lena, Laney, Theo, Margot and Kevin go up Highway 1 on Saturday — Bixby Bridge, Nepenthe, the New
Camaldoli Hermitage (Lena wants the monks' fruitcake and Vespers; Margot wants to see "a functioning patriarchy in its
natural habitat"), a breakdown near Gorda, a motel with one working heater. The ordinariate argument happens in the car
(she's thrilled; he's suspicious of "converts by the busload" — then concedes, or not). Sunday Oct 25: the Saints, down
24–3 at Miami, come back to win 46–34 on an AM station fading in and out through the canyons — Lena screaming at a radio
on the coast road. Opens Thursday with Evan reading Kierkegaard aloud in section until his voice goes. (Theo/Rafi Tijuana
fork cut; the box moved to E10.)

**E12 · "All Hallows' Eve"** · Fri Oct 30 – Sat Oct 31. Las Olas Halloween: 30,000 people in costume on the bluffs. Jack
and Lena end up in accidentally complementary costumes (he's Binx Bolling — no one gets it — she's Flannery O'Connor with
a stuffed peacock; Laney goes as "Sexy Pope," which causes an incident). **Sol glimpsed** filming the chaos with a Bolex, up from LA with Theo's LA crowd; Theo, seeing her: "Soledad. Don't." (🔒 S3). Evan sees Theo and
goes white. Laney missing — the "Point walk" pledge rumor — panic; Jack finds Theo at the wall at 2 a.m. instead.
Midseason pivot.

**E13 · "The Book of the Dead"** · Sun Nov 1 – Tue Nov 3 · All Saints (Sun); All Souls (Mon): Newman's Book of the Dead.
Lena writes *Lucille Guidry* and *Nora Calloway*. "She was my host at Preview Weekend." The monarchs arrive in the Grove.
Theo's 19th birthday (Nov 3) — he takes Jack (and whoever the path allows) to LA: dinner at Caroline's in La Cañada, the
burn scar on the mountains behind the house, Poppy, Richard's no-show. Maine's Question 1 passes the same night; Kevin's
face.

**E14 · "Say It to My Face"** · Wed Nov 4 – Sun Nov 8 · Fort Hood (Nov 5); the House passes health-care reform with the
Stupak amendment (Sat night, Nov 7). Hale's seminar dinner party at his bungalow: magnificent, then vile. Jack and Lena
discover they both have the postcard. Hale, drunk, at the door: "I wrote to you both because you were the only ones who
wrote back like you meant it." Margot's sting to catch the fire-alarm puller fails. **After the postcards, Jack invites Lena to Youngstown for
Thanksgiving** (the Greyhound, three days, "it'll be awful"); she wants to go — her face says so — and says she'll ask
(🔒 a long shot: Wes, her mother, Tess's due date).

**E15 · "Black Monday"** · Mon Nov 9 – Sat Nov 14 · Berlin Wall 20th (Nov 9). Home trouble: Frank's SUB pay is done; he's
drinking; a call from Maria at 1 a.m. Pacific. Jack's money crisis — he can't make the spring housing deposit. Rafi offers
him an "errand" job. Theo offers to just pay. Jack's pride. (Fork: Rafi, Theo, or a third job at the library — each with
costs.) Gina's bullying peaks.

**E16 · "Stairwell"** · Sun Nov 15 – Sat Nov 21 · *New Moon* opens (Nov 20; Laney forces the floor); the Manhattan
Declaration (Nov 20; Lena signs online; Jack argues about it, or signs too). Kevin's episode: his mask cracks at an
orgo study group; Jack half-hears the stairwell fight between Kevin and Theo: "You said you'd never—" "I haven't."
**Lena can't come to Youngstown** ("Mama asked. She never asks."; Wes is meeting her at Louis Armstrong) — **Jack invites
Theo**, who says yes before Jack finishes the sentence ("I'll bring a hostess gift. What do you people drink, Folgers?").
**Becca comes to the *New Moon* night** Laney forces on the floor — Becca loves it, sincerely; Laney is rude to her on
principle; Lena, in the row behind, tells Laney to be nice.

**E17 · "Christ the King"** · Sun Nov 22 – Wed Nov 25 · Christ the King (Nov 22). Nonna is in St. Elizabeth's after a fall;
Jack has to go home now, not by Greyhound; he can't afford the flight; Theo buys **two** tickets over Jack's objection
("I was invited. Guests bring something."). Lena's flight to New Orleans. The airport scene at SBA — all three leaving;
Theo finds an urgent reason to buy a magazine; it's the first goodbye. Saints 10–0.

**E18 · "Thanksgiving"** · Thu Nov 26 – Sun Nov 29 · Thanksgiving in Youngstown; First Sunday of Advent (Year C begins). The
Costigans on-screen in full: the house on Glenwood, Gina's eye-roll, Rosie's drawings, Frank in the garage with the space
heater, Maria's lasagna next to the turkey. **Trumbull Correctional visiting room: Danny.** Nonna in the hospital:
*"Tuo fratello porta la tua croce."* **Theo in Youngstown** (brief: if Lena doesn't go, Theo does): Maria feeds him like a rescue animal; Gina roasts him and
is instantly, mortifyingly smitten; Rosie draws him as a prince; Frank and Theo in the garage with the space heater and
Sinatra — Frank likes him against his will; at Trumbull Theo isn't on the visitor list and waits two hours in the lot in
the Silverado with Frank (🔒 two men keeping secrets, neither knowing the other's; they talk about the Browns). Thanksgiving
night phone call to Lena (Theo hands Jack the phone and leaves the room — Team Lena): the Broussards on speaker (Tony, Luc, Remy,
Josie screaming about the Saints, 10–0, and the Patriots on Monday night), then Lena alone in her childhood bedroom, quiet: "My mama
worked at Memorial." Nothing more. Tiger Woods' car crash on the TV the next morning.

**E19 · "Advent"** · Mon Nov 30 – Sat Dec 5 · Obama's West Point speech (Dec 1: 30,000 troops to Afghanistan). Back at
Marisol. Wes stops answering (🔒; Jack reads it as finals). Lena is brittle. Evan, after section: "Your roommate — was he
at Preview Weekend last April?" **Becca becomes Jack's girlfriend** ("My mom's been calling you my boyfriend since September. Can I tell her she's
right?") — the Newman table cheers; Theo is polite; Laney is not; Lena is lovely to Becca, which is unbearable. *Jersey Shore* premieres (Dec 3); Theo loves it with a terrible sincerity.

**E20 · "Immaculate"** · Sun Dec 6 – Tue Dec 8 · Immaculate Conception (Tues, holy day of obligation). Restraint scene #2:
the rain, the Newman courtyard, an inch — and now Jack has a girlfriend, so the inch is fidelity too. The jacket again: the Fresno Fair pin. Doug Pruitt caught sleepwalking to the
fire alarm (comic payoff; everyone owes Margot five dollars).

**E21 · "Guadalupe"** · Wed Dec 9 – Sat Dec 12 · Our Lady of Guadalupe (Sat): the dawn *Mañanitas* at St. Brendan's with
mariachi. That night, the east stairwell: Wes ends it. Jack sits with her until dawn. He does not kiss her. **The tragedy of
timing:** she is free for the first time since the porch, and Jack has had a girlfriend for two weeks (Becca, who did
nothing wrong). (The player can try — it would be a betrayal of Becca, and the outline prices it; Lena's resolve is high
and her heart is shattered; she stops him gently — "You have a girl, Jack. A nice one." — or he stops himself.)

**Saints ledger** (brief: Lena's weekly religion within a religion; scores per period sheet, some UNVERIFIED): E02 the WHO
DAT shirt; the chant on the beach (nine days out) · E03 the countdown in shoe polish on the Odyssey's back window ("WHO
DAT · 8 DAYS," then 7), the Saturday 53-man cut-down, LSU's opener at Washington on Richard's television with Remy texting
in capitals (score UNVERIFIED — use only if verified) · E05 Sun Sep 13, Detroit, W 45–27, Brees six TD passes [verify] (Sully's; the Odyssey glass goes from "WHO DAT · TODAY"
to "1–0" and becomes the record) · E06 Sep 20
at Philadelphia W · E07 Sep 27 at Buffalo W · E08 Oct 4 Jets W (4–0) · E09 bye week (Wes visits; they talk Saints all night)
· E11 Oct 25 at Miami (the radio in Big Sur) · E13 Mon Nov 2 Atlanta, Monday night, after the Book of the Dead · E15–E16 Nov 8
/ Nov 15 · E17 Nov 22 at Tampa (10–0) · E18 the Thanksgiving call (the Patriots on Monday) · E19 Mon Nov 30 Patriots (11–0) ·
E20 Dec 6 Washington in overtime (12–0) · E22 Dec 13 at Atlanta (13–0, Gaudete Sunday) · Sat Dec 19 Dallas, the first loss
(Christmas break; she texts Jack one word).

**E22 · "Gaudete"** · Sun Dec 13 – Fri Dec 18 · Gaudete Sunday (rose vestments); finals. Hale's oral exam (Jack and Lena,
back to back; Hale asks each of them the other's best argument). The Newman Christmas party. Margot lights a candle "for
research." **The season's strong moment (brief: bittersweet — a big moment between them, every reason for hope, a real sting
that it's not going to work right now; not together):** after the party, **the Delgado roof again** (E07's roof; the solstice
— the longest night — is next week, on Margot's birthday): Lena, free and six days out of Wes; Jack, Becca's boyfriend of
three weeks. The sentence she left unfinished at the lifeguard tower and on the roof in September is finished — by her, or by
him, per path — or so nearly finished that it doesn't matter, and they agree, out loud, that it is not now. She gives him the
Reliquary to keep over Christmas ("Count them. If there's one more when I get back, I'll know."). Then, the last night, her
laptop: the photo of Nora in the jean jacket. "They never found it." **Smash to black.**

## 23. SUMMER AND LATER SEASONS (broad strokes)

**S2 — Spring 2010 (Jan 11 – May 14).** *Title theme: "Do you also wish to go away?"* Haiti (Jan 12). Jack checks the
closet: the duffel is gone. **S2E01 is the Youngstown Christmas** (brief; Dec 24, 2009 – Jan 3, 2010; arc #64): Glenwood
Avenue, Midnight Mass at St. Brigid's, the Trumbull visiting room on the 27th, Nonna's good and bad days; the tin in Jack's
pocket; Lena on the phone from Dupre Street (the Saints' first loss Dec 19; baby Lucille's baptism). **Jack and Becca** (the
trying; Laney's principled rudeness; Lena being nice). **Lena's rebound
is Gideon** (S2E02–E12, her magnetic guy; Theo openly rude, Jack's quiet thanks, Laney polite and feeding Theo) —
**proxy fight #1 (S2E08)**; the near-miss and the end at the line (E10, E12). **Saints win Super Bowl XLIV (Feb 7)** — the
floor watches in the Fishbowl; Lena on a table; Laney in a borrowed Brees jersey; **Mardi Gras in New Orleans (Feb
12–16)**: Jack, Lena, Laney and Theo stay with the Broussards (Becca home in Idaho for the long weekend; Gideon on a
picket); Celeste adopts Laney; Tony takes Jack's measure with a crawfish boil and a Sazerac; Wes at the Zulu parade, kind
and lost; Ash Wednesday (Feb 17) at Holy Rosary. Chile quake (Feb 27) — Sol on the news via Theo (her mother's family in
Concepción). **Becca ends it (S2E15, Lent: "I did everything right").** The Affordable Care Act signed (Mar 23); **Lena
and Julian begin (S2E15–E16).** Lena debates Hale publicly at the Marisol Forum and wins (E16). Easter Vigil (Apr 3).
Priya opens the journal. The one-year anniversary at the Point (Apr 25–26, Good Shepherd Sunday): Evan confronts Theo
before dawn. Deepwater Horizon (Apr 20) — Lena's uncle's shrimp boat in Plaquemines. **E21:** Jack tells Theo about
Danny. **E22:** Theo tells Jack about the Point.

**SU1 — Summer 2010 (6 episodes).** Jack home in Youngstown: Frank rehired for the Cruze launch at Lordstown (the line
restarts — a rare good thing); Jack works a scrapyard and coaches CYO; visits Danny; Nonna's lucid days and bad days;
Carly Benedetto. Lena in New Orleans: the oil spill, the shrimp boats tied up, her uncle's despair; Luc and the Danziger
indictments (July 2010). Phone calls, letters, one visit (Jack takes the Greyhound to New Orleans for the Fourth of July —
or doesn't). Theo in La Cañada, clean for thirty days, then not. The well is capped (July 15).

**S3 — Fall 2010 (Aug 30 – Dec 17).** Sophomores. Jack is a Delgado Hall RA (money) — the east stairwell is his now. Lena and Laney live in Serra Hall, 3rd floor, two doors from 3B, which Lena requested and has told no one why. **Sol**
(Jack's magnetic girl, a transfer from Theo's LA orbit) and **Julian** (Lena's tryer): fire without faith on his side,
faith-respect without fire on hers — the mirrored season. Laney rude to Sol in public, privately likes her; Lena can't
stand her; Theo knows exactly what she is. **Proxy fight #2 (S3E09).** Jack tells Theo to stop on Julian (S3E12); Margot:
"He's the healthiest thing in her life." Sol's darkroom near-miss (S3E14; never sex). Kevin comes out to Jack and Lena.
Kenzie pregnant. Laney's overdose (Nov 20, 2010) — Lena finds her; Theo's cocaine; rehab in Arizona over the break.
Thanksgiving in New Orleans — the Broussards on-screen, Julian at the table (Sol in LA with her father; Jack comes with
Theo), Jack and Lena in the kitchen washing pots at 1 a.m. Lena tells Jack about the test (E18) — he can't tell her what
he knows. Both endings: Sol (E19, Chile or Jack ends it), Julian (E21). **E22 — the first kiss**, at the end of everything,
in the Delgado stairwell on the last night before break, Gaudete week. It is not triumphant. It is two people out of
reasons.

**S4 — Spring 2011 (Jan 10 – May 13).** Together, with a secret between them. The best and worst season. Laney back from
Arizona for E01, newly sober. **S4E03: the alliance reveal** at the Super Bowl XLV party (Feb 6, 2011) — Theo's champagne,
Laney's sparkling cider, "We know." / "Everyone knows." / "People outside of California know." / "It was obvious after five
minutes." **S4E06: one of them names what the proxy fights were really about** (default Lena: "It was never Greene."), and
Jack's fear about Gideon is answered ("Never. Not with anyone."). Nonna's death and funeral in Youngstown (Lena comes;
meets Danny in the visiting room). The resolve season: both low by March. Margot's reporting. Evan goes to the Calloways.
Kevin decides. **Easter Sunday Apr 24**; **Apr 26: Theo surrenders.** Lena learns Jack knew. The rupture — and, if the
player's path made it so, the dangerous night (their first time, with each other) that happened (or didn't) the week
before, now weaponized by guilt. Hale's forty-one seconds come out. Breakup at the Point (E22).

**SU2 — Summer 2011 (5 episodes).** Apart. (Becca marries her Veritas missionary in Coeur d'Alene in June, at
twenty; Jack is invited and goes, or sends a gift — path.) Jack in Youngstown, Lena in New Orleans. The Cloyne Report (July 13) and Enda
Kenny's speech (July 20) make the seal of confession front-page news. **Aug 29, 2011: Wes dies.** Lena calls Jack at 3
a.m. for the first time since April. He gets on a plane.

**S5 — Fall 2011 (Aug 29 – Dec 16).** Juniors. Wes's funeral (S5E01) in New Orleans. The seal on trial: Fr. Osei
subpoenaed; contempt; five nights in county jail. Margot vs. Jack: the war. Theo's plea. Rosa Calloway at Communion. Barron's *Catholicism* airs on PBS (from Oct 2011): Jack watches it in the
Newman lounge every week; Lena comes for the Chartres episode and stays.
Danny's judicial release (Nov 2011) if Jack's path supports it. Sol returns with a film. Lena dates a law-school-bound
Jesuit-educated guy who's perfect on paper (Andrew) — another person who wants something else (kissing at most; hard rule;
Theo rude, Laney polite, the alliance in exile mode).

**S6 — Spring 2012 (Jan – May).** Repair. Osei's limp story. Jack's priesthood flirtation (a real discernment weekend at
St. Patrick's Seminary, Menlo Park) — and Lena's reaction. The HHS mandate fight (Jan–Feb 2012) — Lena and Jack on the
same side for once, Margot against them both, Kevin in the middle; it rattles Lena's left lean. **Super Tuesday, Mar 6,
2012:** the Ohio primary — Jack's absentee ballot (Santorum, room's pick: Catholic, pro-life, Rust Belt; Romney narrowly
wins Ohio), Frank on the phone from Glenwood Avenue; Lena's material on Gingrich's marriages. Hale's cirrhosis. Jack and Lena, again — slowly,
eyes open.

**SU3 — Summer 2012.** The Fortnight for Freedom (June–July 2012); the Supreme Court upholds the ACA (June 28). **Paul Ryan
named Romney's running mate (Aug 11, 2012):** the subsidiarity-vs.-solidarity duel (the bishops' and Georgetown faculty's
public criticism of his budget). Jack meets
the Broussards as Lena's boyfriend, officially; Tony and Jack go fishing; Celeste and Jack talk in the kitchen. Lena meets
Danny out of prison (if released).

**S7 — Fall 2012 (Aug – Dec).** Seniors. **The Biden–Ryan VP debate watch party (Oct 11, 2012)** — two Catholics on
abortion; the Fishbowl. **S7E09 "Subject": the Ephesians 5 conversation** (§24.5). The 2012 election (Nov 6): the duel to
end all duels and considerable comedy — **Jack votes Romney** and is the most hated man at the election-night party
(Margot, the campus, firmly Obama); **Lena reluctantly votes Obama** after agonizing (third party, a write-in, both
considered on the page). Jack tells Donna
Kostelnik the truth; then the prosecutor. The dangerous nights are possible again — they're in love, they're adults,
they're exhausted. Whatever happened earlier is part of who they are now.

**S8 — Spring 2013 (Jan – May).** **Feb 11, 2013: Benedict XVI resigns** — it lands hard on Jack (he loves him); Lena,
who respects him without being enthralled, is surprised by how much it hurts him and sits up with him; they argue at
3 a.m. about whether a pope may simply stop (Celestine V). **Mar 13: Francis** — Lena adores him on sight (the peripheries,
the bus, the shoes); Jack likes him and is uneasy, and the durable argument begins. Easter Vigil (Mar 30): Laney received; Theo confirmed (path). Hale's
last lecture as commencement speaker. Danny home. **Graduation (May 2013).** A ring — in the Newman chapel at 5 a.m., or at
the Point, or at the Anchor — player's choice.

**Adulthood (AD).** 2013–: Lena at Tulane Law, Jack at Notre Dame for an MA, then home to teach at Mooney. The wedding in
New Orleans at Holy Rosary (summer 2014 or 2015 by path), Osei presiding, Theo best man, Laney maid of honor, Julian
playing, Margot as "witness for the prosecution," Kwame in the back. Hale's death (2015). Celeste's confession at the
rehearsal dinner. Children; money; Danny as godfather; the closing of Lordstown (March 2019) and Theo's film about it;
Frank's last day. *Laudato Si'* (June 2015): Lena's joy. Barron made auxiliary bishop of Los Angeles (2015). The
Francis argument, durable and affectionate and sometimes heated (Jack: he's held the line on doctrine despite the
off-the-cuff remarks; but how he treats the conservative cardinals Jack admires — real public events only). **Nov 8,
2016:** probably the last time Jack votes Republican and the last time Lena votes Democrat; both mad at each other, madder at
themselves — a marriage-scale fight with shame underneath, not a cartoon. After: both politically homeless. The Trump years
are hard on Jack (immigration, enforcement turning to dehumanization); Lena grows dissatisfied with Barron and Jack defends
him as doing the best he can (Winona-Rochester, 2022). **Dobbs
(June 24, 2022)**: joy for both, then ash. **May 8, 2025: Leo XIV** — both of them, for once, in complete agreement; play it
as joy and relief, maybe a reconciliation.

## 24. THE ROMANCE ROADMAP — restraint is the fire

### 24.1 Shape
- **S1:** rivals → friends → the two people who stay. She's taken; he's honorable; the heat is all proximity and argument.
  Two restraint scenes (E10 library, E20 courtyard) and one test (E21 stairwell). **No kiss.** Late in the season Jack
  says yes to Becca (E19), believing Wes is forever — so when Wes ends it (E21), Jack is unavailable: **the tragedy of
  timing** (brief).
- **S2:** apart in other arms. Jack + Becca (the tryer: faith and effort, no fire); Lena + Gideon (her magnetic guy: fire,
  no faith), then Julian. Jealousy as POV; **proxy fight #1** (E08); Mardi Gras, a near-miss on Fat Tuesday night in the
  Broussards' back courtyard (E07: neither moves; Wes's voice in the front room). Becca's sad ending (E15).
- **S3:** the mirror season. Jack + Sol (fire without faith); Lena + Julian (faith-respect without fire). **Proxy fight
  #2** (E09). The kiss at the end, after both endings. Only Lena has both; only Jack has both.
- **S4:** together. The honeymoon (weeks of argument and kissing and fear); the line held in public and tested in
  private; the danger windows; the rupture.
- **S5:** apart; Wes's funeral; grace through grief.
- **S6:** repair — slow, explicit about forgiveness.
- **S7–S8:** together, adults, the line chosen again (or re-chosen after it broke), the ring.
- **AD:** marriage — the show does not end at the wedding; married love is the hardest saying.
- **S1 cadence (player's standing rule after S1E04):** an **extended Jack/Lena two-hander roughly every other episode** —
  E04 (the harbor, played), **E06** (the long way home from Pi Lam's barn party: the beach, the creek mouth, the lifeguard
tower, the Lagoon bench — played), *E07 (no two-hander; the Delgado roof at dawn after Margot's wake, ~1,800 words, and the
friendship duel — Lena central)*, **E08** (the player's own design: the Thérèse night, Wed–Thu — the laundry room, *Story of a Soul*, Lupe's, Shipwreck Cove under a setting moon; scrupulous friends → the crack → one of their best nights → "nice… sweet" → her relief → guilt at the Delgado elevator; the retreat that never takes, #66), **E10** (the
  library at 4 a.m., restraint #1), **E12/E13** (shift by one around the Halloween setpiece: All Souls, "She was my host"),
  **E14** (the postcards, after Hale's dinner), **E16**, **E18** (the Thanksgiving phone call, Dupre Street to Glenwood
  Avenue), **E20** (the courtyard, restraint #2), **E21** (the stairwell until dawn). **Ensemble setpieces as punctuation**:
  E03 (convoy), **E05 (Sully's)**, E08 (Blessing of the Animals: Osei meets Hale), E11 (Big Sur), E12 (Halloween), E14 (Hale's
  dinner party), E17/E18, E22 (Newman Christmas party). Lena central every week regardless.
- **The S1 rhythm after E08 (player's design):** every big near is followed by a retreat — scrupulous, polite, painful — that
  fails faster each time (E08 a few days, then about five minutes), into *guilty fun*: their best hours together cost Becca and
  Wes something neither is told. Jack's guilt is the S1 engine the player feels most; keep it alive whenever Becca is in a scene
  (her goodness is the knife, never her foolishness). The retreat only takes once it is too late (E19, Becca official) — the
  timing tragedy, felt from the inside.

### 24.2 Who holds the line, when (Lena's resolve is set by writers)
| Season | Jack's resolve tendency | Lena's resolve (writer-set baseline) | Who holds |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | Mid (party, Theo, smoking) | High (7–8), with dips: E09 Wes leaves (6), E21 breakup (4) | **Lena** — and in E21 she's low, so **Jack** must |
| S2 | Mid (Becca keeps him steady; Theo drags; guilt) | **Low-mid** after Wes (4–6; Gideon drains her; Mardi Gras 5) | **Jack** (and Lena herself at the Gideon line, E10) |
| S3 | **Low** (Sol, Theo's secret) | Mid (Julian steady; Laney's OD drops her to 3 for two episodes) | **Lena**, then neither (E16) |
| S4 | Mid → low (secret, Nonna's death) | **Low** (guilt, love, Laney's rehab) — 3–4 in E10–E19 | **Neither, at times** — the danger windows |
| S5 | Variable | Very low after Wes; then rising | Apart |
| S6–S8 | Player-driven | 6–8, rising with confession and Mass | Both, or not; the player decides the shape |

### 24.3 The dangerous nights (only possible when `resolve.jack<=3 && resolve.lena<=3`)
1. **S3E16** (the week after Laney's overdose, Nov 2010 — they're with other people; if it happens it's a betrayal of
   Julian and Sol, and the show treats it that way). Lena's resolve is written at 3–4 here; the player can only reach the
   window if Jack's resolve has cratered *and* the writers drop Lena's in-episode. Default: not reachable.
**Hard rule (brief):** every dangerous night is between Jack and Lena. With anyone else (Becca, Sol, Gideon, Julian,
anyone) the ceiling is the near-miss, regardless of resolve: low resolve changes how close it gets and what it costs,
never whether the line holds (§24.8).
2. **S4E12** (Nonna's funeral, Youngstown, the Costigan basement, after the wake's whiskey). Lena at 3. The likeliest.
3. **S4E19** (the night before Theo surrenders; neither knows what's coming; Jack knows). Lena at 3.
4. **S7E10** (Election night 2012). Adults, together, tired. Lena at 4.
If it happens: frank, literary, specific, tender and frightening; then the morning, the guilt, the confession lines at St.
Brendan's on Saturday at 3:30 (both, separately, neither knowing), the rupture and the repair. It never destroys them. It
changes them. If it never happens, the wedding night is written (AD) with the full weight of the wait, and the show is
honest that waiting had costs too.

### 24.4 How the meters bend the route (never break endgame)
- **Endgame is fixed:** they marry. What the player shapes is *how*, *when*, *at what cost*, and *who they are when they
  get there*.
- **High `rel.lena.trust`** shortens the S4 rupture (she forgives sooner; S5 has fewer episodes apart). **Low trust**
  lengthens it into S6 and gives Andrew (S5) real weight.
- **High `rel.lena.heat` with low `rel.lena.trust`**: the relationship becomes about the line, not the person; S4 is
  feverish; the rupture is worse.
- **High `rel.lena.friction`**: more duels, sharper; Lena wins more; the relationship is a fire that needs feeding.
  Friction is not bad — some friction is how they love.
- **`soul.grace`** governs whether Jack goes to confession after a fall, and how fast; **`soul.honesty`** governs when
  he tells Lena the truth about Theo (high honesty → he tells her before Theo surrenders and loses her anyway, but the
  repair is faster); **`soul.restraint`** governs the danger windows and Sol.
- **Lena's other relationships** run regardless of the player; Jack's choices color them (a jealous Jack makes Julian's
  arc sadder; a generous Jack becomes Julian's friend, and Julian's ending is cleaner).
- **Jack's other relationships**: **Becca** (S1E06–S2E15, the tryer; fixed in shape — the player shapes how warm, how honest
  and how it ends, not whether it happens, because the brief's timing tragedy needs her in place by E21); **Sol** (S3; the player can keep it at flirtation — then S3 is lonelier and his resolve dips through
  Theo and Theo's secret instead — but she arrives and the pull is real). Neither ever goes past the near-miss.

### 24.5 The Ephesians 5 conversation (reserved; brief directive)
- **When:** S7E09 "Subject" (late Oct 2012), not before they are seriously together. It can play as a full duel and define
  the episode.
- **Seeds only before then:** S1E06 Margot reads Eph 5:22–24 at the chastity talk Q&A; the Sunday before the pilot (Aug 23,
  2009) had Eph 5:21–32 as the second reading, so both heard it at home the week they left — a callback either can spring;
  S3E17 Peter and Kenzie's wedding uses the reading and Jack and Lena exchange a look and table it.
- **Texts:** Eph 5:21–33 ("Be subject to one another out of reverence for Christ"; "Husbands, love your wives, as Christ
  loved the church and gave himself up for her"); *Mulieris Dignitatem* §24 (mutual subjection); John Paul II's Theology of
  the Body audiences on Ephesians 5 (1982); *Casti Connubii* (1930); headship as service and sacrifice vs. as authority.
- **What each of them hears (don't assume sides):** 🔒 Jack's father exercised headship once that mattered — he decided,
  alone, that Danny would carry Jack's cross. Jack may argue against authority-headship from inside that wound, or defend it
  as the terrible cost of being the one who decides. 🔒 Lena's mother followed orders onto a boat and left her own mother
  upstairs; at home Tony's loud headship is warm theater and Celeste runs everything in a whisper. Lena may argue mutual
  subjection as the only safe kind, or argue that she wants a man who will carry a decision so she never has to be the
  woman on the stairs. Both intelligent, personal, surprising. Margot's critique is setup only; the conversation is theirs.

### 24.6 The proxy fights (reserved, recurring; brief)
While either magnetic attraction is live (Lena + Gideon, S2; Jack + Sol, S3 — the periods don't overlap, so at least one
in each), Jack and Lena have a **serious fight with each other that is 100% about the other person being with someone
else, and neither can say it.** Vicious, specific, personal; the subtext obvious to the player and to Theo, Laney and Margot
(who exchange looks and say nothing — alliance discipline); the real reason **never spoken** by either during the period.
Play as argument duels where *winning the stated argument is the wrong thing to win* (a Press/Personal win costs trust and
raises friction; a Concede is the only move that helps, and it doesn't feel like it).
1. **S2E08 (late Feb 2010, first week of Lent; Lena with Gideon, Jack with Becca):** ostensibly Dorothy Day and the Harbor
   Inn strike — Jack calls her "a tourist in the class war"; she calls him "a company man for the company that laid off
   his father." Frank is used as a weapon. Both say the cruelest true thing they know. Margot, after: "Well. That wasn't
   about the housekeepers."
2. **S3E09 (Oct 2010; Jack with Sol, Lena with Julian):** ostensibly Greene's *The End of the Affair* in an upper-division
   seminar (literature as moral test: is Sarah's sanctity real or a writer's alibi for adultery?) — Lena savages the book,
   Jack defends it; it turns into how he's "changing" (the skipped Mass for a dawn shoot) and whether she's dating a man who
   agrees with her because she can't stand to lose. Laney, to Theo: "Sugar, that was not about a *book*."
**Payoff:** S4E06, once they're together, one of them finally names what those fights were (default Lena: "It was never
Greene. It was never the housekeepers."). Path may hand the line to Jack, or slide it to S6's repair.

### 24.7 The secret alliance and its reveal (brief)
Theo and Laney, mirrored, from S1E07: **Laney** openly rude to all Jack's girls and dates (Lena tells her to be nice to all
but Sol), privately feeding Theo; **Theo** polite to Jack's girls, privately Team Lena; **Theo** openly rude to Lena's men
after Wes (not Wes), Jack mostly letting him (thanks him on Gideon; stops him on Julian, S3E12); **Laney** polite to Lena's
men, privately Team Jack. **Kevin** objects every season; **Margot** likes Julian. Their maneuvering is visible (seating
charts, a borrowed car that won't start, a party invitation that reaches only one of them, a "lost" phone) and **never
acknowledged aloud** — years of comic evidence. **Reveal: S4E03** (Super Bowl XLV, Feb 6, 2011; the first ensemble setpiece
after Jack and Lena are together): champagne, the toast, and: "We know." / "Everyone knows." / "People outside of California
know." / "It was obvious after five minutes." (player's lines; keep them verbatim).

### 24.8 Other-relationship ledger (hard rule caps)
| Who | With | When | Ceiling | How the line holds |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lena | Wes | before–S1E21 | kissing | they hold it together (his idea as much as hers) |
| Jack | Becca | S1E06–S2E15 | kissing, warmth | she's waiting too; nothing deeper there ("I did everything right") |
| Lena | Gideon | S2E04–E12 | making out; the near-miss (E10) | she stops; "Then what are we doing, Counselor?" |
| Lena | Julian | S2E15–S3E21 | kissing | he never asks; that's the problem and the grace |
| Jack | Sol | S3 | making out; the darkroom near-miss (E14) | she stops it, or he does; resolve sets *how* |
| Lena | Andrew | S5 | kissing | wants something else |
Their first time is with each other — S4 danger windows, S7, or the wedding night — as the player's choices make it.

# PART IV — THE WORLD

## 25. California State University at Marisol

**Where.** A fictional town of 38,000 (plus 24,000 students) on California's Central Coast, on a marine terrace where the
coast bends west, roughly between San Luis Obispo and Santa Barbara. Highway 1 / US-101 runs inland of town; the Amtrak
Pacific Surfliner and the Coast Starlight run along the bluff (the 2:10 a.m. freight horn is the show's clock). Nearest
real airport: Santa Barbara (SBA), fifty-five minutes south; LAX is three hours by car or a Surfliner-and-FlyAway
odyssey. San Francisco: five hours north.

**Climate and light.** Late summer: fog in the morning ("the marine layer"), burning off by eleven; 70s by afternoon; fog
back by dusk; 55–58°F at night. September and October are the warmest, clearest months (Indian summer, Santa Ana days
when the wind turns and the air smells like hot sage and fire). November rains begin. Winter: green hills, storms,
big surf. Monarch butterflies overwinter in the eucalyptus Grove from late October to February.

**Reputation.** Elite public flagship: top-15 public university, Nobel laureates in physics and economics, a ferocious
honors college, a party reputation it hates and earns. Intensely secular and progressive. About 7% of students identify as
practicing Catholics; Newman's Sunday attendance is about 600 across five Masses.

### 25.1 Campus geography (north to south, east to west)
- **The Lagoon** (north edge): a brackish lagoon with a footpath, egrets, and a bench where people go to break up.
- **The Anacapa residential complex** (northeast, on the lagoon): four freshman halls — **Delgado**, Cabrillo, Portolá, and
  Serra (Serra houses sophomores and Preview Weekend overflow).
- **The Commons Lawn** (center): the quad. Big lawn, Frisbees, sunbathers, protest tables, the **Ferrer Tower** (a 1966
  carillon tower that plays the Westminster chimes off-key on the quarter hour and "Hotel California" on the last day of
  finals by tradition).
- **Hollis Hall** (west side of the Commons): the only 1920s building, Spanish Revival, red tile, a cloister walk with
  bougainvillea; Philosophy, Classics, Religious Studies. **Hale's office: Hollis 314** (third floor, a window on the sea,
  a carpet with a wine stain shaped like Italy). Seminar room **Hollis 302** (PHIL 9H; long oak table, twelve chairs,
  a radiator that knocks). **Hollis 108** (Evan's section; a basement room with one window at ankle height).
- **Sandoval Hall 1** (east of the Commons): a 1971 lecture hall, 400 orange flip-down seats, a stage, a wireless mic Hale
  refuses to clip on. PHIL 1 lectures Tue/Thu 9:30–10:45.
- **Anacapa Commons** (the freshman dining hall, between Delgado and Serra): salad bar, a waffle iron with a line, Kevin's
  protein ratios.
- **Pacifica Library** ("the Brick"; south side of the Commons): eight-story brutalist tower; the 8th floor is silent
  study with a view of the Channel Islands on clear days; the 24-hour study room in the basement during finals.
- **Sciences** (east): the Chem building where Kevin lives in his head; the **Marine Lab** on the beach below the east
  bluff with the touch tank (Becca borrows a hermit crab from it, E08).
- **The Student Union** ("the UCen"): a Panda Express, a Jamba Juice, the *Marisol Tide* newsroom upstairs (Margot).
- **Campus Point** (south): the beach below the bluff; a surf break for beginners; the bluff-top bench where Jack smokes.
- **The Grove** (west, between campus and Las Olas): a eucalyptus forest; the bike path cuts through it; monarchs in
  winter; at night the trees creak and drop bark like shed skin.
- **The creek mouth** (added E06 plan): the Lagoon's outlet creek runs under the Grove and crosses the beach below it,
  between the Las Olas bluff stairs and Campus Point; shin-deep and cold, wider after rain; at night you hear it before you
  see it. The beach route from Las Olas to campus has to wade it. (🔒 Theo crossed it, soaked to the knees, on his way from
  the Point to Serra Hall, ~12:40 a.m. April 26, 2009.) A lifeguard tower (closed after Labor Day) stands on the sand at
  Campus Point; the Marine Lab stairs climb the bluff beside it.

### 25.2 Las Olas (the student town, west of the Grove)
A square mile of bluff-top rentals, duplexes and surf houses — dense, filthy, gorgeous, loud. Bikes everywhere. Couches on
roofs. Jasmine and trash cans. The ocean at the end of every street.
- **Seacliff Road** runs along the bluff edge to the Point. **Greek row** is its eastern half: Pi Lambda Chi (white
  colonial, black shutters, the best porch), Kappa Alpha Delta, Sigma Rho (the lacrosse house), Alpha Tau Kappa. West of the
  Greek houses Seacliff becomes surf rentals.
- **The Lighthouse** is the last house on Seacliff Road before the Point: a shingled 1950s two-story with a deck and a
  back porch that looks north to the Point and the old light, rented each year by a new crew of senior surfers; named for
  the view. A chain-link fence at the back of the lot; a gap where the fence post rotted; beyond it, a dirt path along the
  bluff to the Point.
- **Del Playa Lane** (Julian's garage studio; Sol's house; Hale's bungalow at the far end on the cliff).
- **Shipwreck Cove**: a beach reached by wooden stairs from the end of Del Playa; bonfire beach; the ribs of a 1920s
  schooner at low tide.

### 25.3 Marisol Point
The westernmost headland: a grassy bluff seventy feet above a reef break (the Point break — expert surfers only), with
the **old Point Marisol Light** (a squat white 1890s Coast Guard light, decommissioned 1967, now a locked shell with a
county sign) and a **low stone wall** (knee-high on the land side, a sheer drop on the sea side). A small dirt lot off the
end of Seacliff Road. The **white wooden cross** at the wall, with Nora's name burned into the crossbar. Fresh flowers every
Sunday. Ice plant, wind, the sound of sea lions from the rocks.

### 25.4 St. Brendan the Navigator Newman Center
Across Ocean Road from campus, at the edge of the Grove. A 1962 A-frame church whose ceiling is a ship's hull turned upside
down (St. Brendan sailed the Atlantic in a leather boat; the architect took it literally) — Douglas-fir ribs, blue
dalle-de-verre glass, a hanging crucifix carved from driftwood. A Marian shrine in the side aisle with a votive rack and,
beneath it, **a small brass plate: NORA GRACE CALLOWAY · 1989–2009 · "LORD, TO WHOM SHALL WE GO?"** A courtyard with an
olive tree, a temperamental sprinkler and folding tables for Coffee & Donuts after the 9:30 and 11:30. The Newman house
(offices, lounge with a 1994 couch, kitchen, the library of donated books, a ping-pong table with a broken net). Osei's
office: highlife, chess board, a framed photo face-down in the drawer. The confessional: a converted coat closet with a
screen and a kneeler and a chair, so you can choose.
- **Mass schedule:** Sat Vigil 5:00 p.m.; Sun 9:30, 11:30, 5:00 and 9:00 p.m. (the college Mass, candlelit); weekdays
  12:10 p.m. **Confession:** Sat 3:30–4:45; Wed 8:00 p.m.; "whenever you knock."

### 25.5 Delgado Hall
1967 concrete, five stories, a grid of narrow windows facing the Lagoon, co-ed by room (alternating doors; men's and
women's bathrooms at each end of each floor). Home to 310 freshmen.
- **Ground floor:** lobby with a front desk (a sleepy desk attendant named Marcus), mailboxes, the **Fishbowl** (the glass-
  walled main lounge with a TV, a broken foosball table, couches that have seen things).
- **Basement:** laundry (eight washers, three working), vending machines, a piano no one admits to playing.
- **2nd floor:** **217 — Lena & Laney** (east wing, window on the Lagoon). **204 — Rafi** (roommate never showed).
- **3rd floor:** **305 — Margot** (single).
- **4th floor:** **412 — Jack & Theo** (west wing, window toward the Grove and, on clear nights, the Point light's dead
  silhouette). **420 — Kevin & Doug.** **401 — Priya (RA).** The 4th-floor lounge (smaller, with a whiteboard — Margot
  annexes it).
- **5th floor:** a door to the roof marked ALARM WILL SOUND (the alarm was disabled in 1998 with a popsicle stick; every
  class passes the secret down).
- **The east stairwell:** concrete, echoing, one window on each landing that opens six inches; best cell reception in the
  building; where people smoke illegally and make phone calls they don't want their roommates to hear. **The 2nd–3rd floor
  landing is Lena's call spot.** Jack, on the 4th floor, uses the same stairwell. Sound carries.
- **The 3 a.m. fire alarm:** about every ten days; everyone stands on the lawn in towels.

### 25.6 Town (Marisol proper, three miles east along Mission Street)
- **Mission Street:** the main drag — palms, Spanish tile, a Borders that's dying, a Blockbuster that's dying faster.
- **Old Mission Nuestra Señora del Mar** (fictional, 1790s adobe): Christmas Midnight Mass, Holy Week, weddings.
- **The Anchor** (the kitchen goes dark one Thursday night a year in September for the hood cleaners — coffee and pie only;
  S1E04): a 24-hour diner on the harbor wharf; Formica, red vinyl booths, a counter with spinning stools, a pie
  case, a jukebox with Sinatra, Otis, Patsy Cline, the Eagles, and inexplicably one Mariah Carey. Gloria Mendes runs the
  night shift; Rudy Salcedo works the line. Jack washes dishes there from S1E02 (Tue/Thu/Sat, 7 p.m.–1 a.m.; the Hobart
  dish machine). The 11 bus stops at 12:30 a.m.; Jack rides home three miles on La Flaca. Pie of the day on a chalkboard; Jack rates them (marionberry 4/10,
  "Dad would complain"; olallieberry 9/10).
- **Café Bastille:** indie coffee on Mission; mismatched chairs, French posters, Thursday open mic (Julian), a barista with
  a Che tattoo who argues with Lena about Che.
- **The Wharf Rat:** a dive bar on the wharf; fishermen, grad students, Hale in the back booth; doesn't card if Gloria
  vouches; a dartboard with a photo of Dick Cheney on it from 2004 that no one has taken down.
- **Sully's:** sports bar on Mission; fake IDs; Saints games (Lena demands it); Browns games (Jack in mourning). **(E05
  canon:)** one big projection screen and five tube TVs; the Sunday-morning crowd is transplants (a Packers table, a Steelers
  table, two Raiders guys who fight each other); Bloody Mary bar at 10 a.m.; no Abita ("Is that a cheese?"); a bell over the
  bar the owner rings for touchdowns until Lena makes him regret it; Dale at the end of the bar.
- **Lupe's:** a taco truck that parks at the Las Olas end of Seacliff from 10 p.m. to 3 a.m.; carnitas, horchata, absolution.
- **Driftwood Books:** used bookstore with two cats (Boethius and Mrs. Danvers); Jack's paperbacks come from here, $2–$4.
  The owner (sixties, glasses on a beaded chain, unnamed) ships books to prisons "as the seller" for families — Jack's
  monthly book to Trumbull goes through her from S1E04; his margin notes go separately by letter.
- **Giorgio's:** pizza by the slice; Jack's culinary despair ("this isn't pizza, this is a cracker with ambitions").
- **The Walgreens on Mission Street.** (🔒 2:14 p.m., April 25, 2009.)
- **Marisol Police Department:** a stucco building on Anacapa Street.
- **Marisol Harbor:** fishing boats, sea lions on the docks, a fish market, the Coast Guard station.

## 26. RUNNING JOKES, MOTIFS, RECURRING OBJECTS

- **The eyebrow scar.** A new story every time (§16). Track tellings. Truth reserved.
- **The Marlboros and the Reliquary.** Lena confiscates every cigarette she catches him with — takes it from his mouth or
  hand, stubs it out, and drops it into an Altoids tin she calls "the Reliquary." She keeps count, out loud. (Pilot: the
  first one goes in her cardigan pocket; the tin appears in E03.) 🔒 At the wedding, she gives him the tin: 211 cigarettes,
  each labeled with a date in her handwriting. **The tin (S1E03):** a red-and-white peppermint Altoids tin bought at the
  gift shop of Pea Soup Andersen's in Buellton on the 101, Sat Sep 5; she pours the mints into Kevin's palm and seals in
  Saturday's cigarette; on the lid in Sharpie, in her courtroom capitals, RELIQUARY, and inside, on a slip of Andersen's
  receipt paper, "No. 1 · 8/29/09 · the Lighthouse." Each later deposit gets its own slip. It lives in her cardigan pocket
  or her boot.
- **The paperback in the back pocket.** A different book most episodes; the narration always names it; Lena reads his
  margins when he isn't looking; his margins argue with the author ("NO." "Coward." "Okay, fine." "Show Lena"). Pilot:
  Walker Percy, *The Moviegoer* (1961, Vintage, spine cracked white, $3 from a Youngstown library sale) — Hale's postcard as
  bookmark. Rotation ideas: Chesterton, *Orthodoxy*; Camus, *The Plague*; O'Connor, *A Good Man Is Hard to Find*; Greene,
  *The Power and the Glory*; Dostoevsky, *The Brothers Karamazov* (too fat for a pocket — it becomes a joke); Hopkins,
  *Poems and Prose*; Pascal, *Pensées*; Kierkegaard, *Fear and Trembling*; Waugh, *Brideshead*; Hale's own book (Jack
  reading it at Mass by accident is an E08 gag). **Played/planned:** E01 *The Moviegoer*; E02 *Spe Salvi*; E03 *Brideshead*
  (→ Danny, E04); **E04 Hopkins, *Poems and Prose*** (Penguin, Gardner; a Mooney library discard) — pencil *Show Lena* beside
  "Thou art indeed just, Lord." **E05 Flannery O'Connor, *A Good Man Is Hard to Find and Other Stories*** (a Harvest paperback
  from the milk crate, a Mooney senior-English castoff); the Benedict holy card moves into it; the Hopkins stays on the desk
  until it goes to Danny in October. The Misfit's "I can't make what all I done wrong fit what all I gone through in
  punishment" is available to Jack in the duel (🔒 Danny). Seeds E12's O'Connor duel and Lena's Halloween costume.
- **The Miraculous Medal.** Lena touches it when thinking **or lying**. The player knows this from their own brief; Jack
  doesn't, until he notices the lie-tell in S2E05 (journal note). Before that, the narration simply records each touch
  without comment. Writers: every medal-touch must be consistent (only when she is genuinely thinking hard or lying) — it is
  a fair-play clue system the player can read before Jack can.
- **The St. Michael medal.** Jack's from his confirmation (Danny was his sponsor — 🔒). He holds it when he prays the St.
  Michael prayer. In S4 he gives it to Theo the night before Theo surrenders.
- **The postcard.** "Come and say it to my face. —A.H." Radcliffe Camera, Oxford. Jack's (2008), Lena's (2008), Nora's
  (2007).
- **The jean jacket.** Fresno Fair pin. Guadalupe patch.
- **The Memorare.** Nora's last prayer; Lena's prayer; Nonna's prayer; Theo knows it without knowing how (he heard it once).
- **John 6:68.** "Lord, to whom shall we go?" On Nora's plaque; in her journal; the last line of the series finale.
- **Bread.** The Anchor's toast; Nonna's bread; Lena's mother's French bread for po'boys; the host. The show's secret
  metaphor; never named in narration.
- **The fog.** Arrives at dusk like a tide. Things happen in it.
- **The 2:10 a.m. freight horn.** Jack counts the cars when he can't sleep.
- **The monarchs.** They arrive at All Souls. They cluster for warmth. Nobody who sees them for the first time talks.
- **Margot's whiteboard.** "USEFUL IDIOTS FOR CHRISTIAN NATIONALISM" — a running tally (J vs. L), later expanded ("PATRIARCHY
  UPHELD THIS WEEK," "INTERNALIZED MISOGYNY: LENA, UNREPENTANT"), with Jack and Lena adding their own categories ("MARGOT:
  FEELINGS DETECTED").
- **"Theodore."** Theo is only called this when he's in trouble. When Jack calls him "Theodore" for the first time with
  real anger, it lands like a slap.
- **Laney's pageant wave**, deployed at funerals and police officers.
- **Priya's laminated notes.** A new laminated sign every episode ("THE MICROWAVE IS NOT A PLACE OF WORSHIP." "PER MY
  EMAIL: NO.").
- **Kevin's quarter-zips.** Color-coded to his mood; when he wears a plain T-shirt something is very wrong.
- **Fr. Osei's sprinkler.** He's always fixing it. It's never fixed. (🔒 In the series finale, it's finally fixed — by Theo.)
- **Hale's wine-cuff.** The linen jacket with the stain. When he shows up in a clean jacket, he's in trouble.
- **The 3 a.m. fire alarm** (S1; Doug, sleepwalking).
- **The Anchor pie ratings.** Jack's chalkboard rankings, which Gloria posts by the register.
- **Gina's texts.** All lowercase, no punctuation, devastating: "nonna asked if u have a girlfriend i said yes shes a nun".
- **Jack's flip phone**: the cracked hinge; the T9; the screen that only lights at an angle.
- **The paired papers.** From S1E02, Hale's designated antagonists: every week Jack and Lena swap two-page papers and
  annotate each other in red. Her margins: "NO." "Cite me." His: "This is the best paragraph I've read this year. I hate
  it." The margins are where they say what they can't say out loud. (Payoff: E14, E22.)
- **La Flaca.** Rafi's rusted single-speed beach cruiser with coaster brakes, lent to Jack "till Christmas" (S1E02): "Not
  stolen. Abandoned. My abuela says there's about a week's difference."
- **The Benedict holy card.** Jack's bookmark in *Spe Salvi* (S1E02); it migrates from book to book all season.
- **The Odyssey's back glass.** E03–E04 the Saints countdown in La Vang car-wash polish; from E05 it carries the record
  ("1–0," and so on), updated by Lena the morning after each game; Kevin pretends to object. It reads 13–0 by Gaudete.
- **The fleur-de-lis.** Lena paints one in gold on Jack's cheekbone at the opener (E05, Laney's eyeliner); he forgets to
  wash it off before Mass. On game days afterward she checks his face like a sacristan checking a candle.
- **Lena's boots.** Brown Justins, resoled twice. When she's wearing Keds, she's happy. When she's barefoot, something's
  wrong. (E06: barefoot on the Pi Lam steps with the boots beside her, and she carries them the whole long way home.)
- **Hale's books.** From E06 Jack carries Hale's own copy of Bernard Williams, *Moral Luck* (Cambridge paperback, Hale's
  Oxford pencil in the margins — "No." "Bernard, really." — a man who argued with his teacher the way Jack argues with his
  authors). "Bring it back with your margins in it." 🔒 Hale is courting him; the book is a leash as much as a gift.
- **Ray's light.** The clip-on bike light from Ray Ochoa's trunk (E06), on La Flaca's handlebar from then on.
- **Danny's letters.** Lined paper, block-capital addresses, "Professor," signed "—D." Jack keeps them in the milk crate
  inside the Hopkins until the Hopkins ships, then inside whatever replaces it.
- **The paperback, E07:** Augustine, *Confessions* (Penguin Classics, Pine-Coffin), CARDINAL MOONEY H.S. LIBRARY — DISCARD,
  bought for a dime ("Augustine for a dime" — Lena, E04); Jack's margins thickest in Book IV (the friend who died). Hale's
  Williams moves to the desk (one pencil *No.* so far — owed back with margins); the Benedict holy card moves into Augustine.
- **The roof door.** Delgado's fifth floor: EMERGENCY EXIT ONLY · ALARM WILL SOUND, and under it, in a Sharpie gone brown,
  *(it won't)*; a popsicle stick in the strike since 1998. The roof: tar and gravel, the elevator housing, two HVAC boxes, a
  parapet to the hip; the Lagoon one way, the hills the other. **E07 (the equinox) and E22 (a week before the solstice) are
  its two big nights** — equal night, longest night (arc #65).
- **Margot's whiteboard, E07:** "TUESDAY — NO SCORE. (BEREAVEMENT.)" — the first time she suspends the tally. Never explained.
- **Hale's Housman for Danny** (E07, `e6_hale_danny`): a wartime pocket *A Shropshire Lad*, foxed, no margins — "They'd only
  erase them." 🔒 kindness and leash.
- **Gloria's people-ratings, E07:** Margot, sight unseen, from Jack's description: "Rhubarb. Tart, and nobody can decide if
  it's a fruit." (It's a vegetable. That's the point.)
- **The paperback, E08: Thérèse of Lisieux, *Story of a Soul*** (ICS Publications, John Clarke's translation — Researcher to confirm
  the edition and page wording), $3 off the Driftwood cart Monday Sep 28, read Monday and Tuesday nights **in pencil** (his margins
  are always pencil — the prison taught him erasable). Margins: *Easy for you.* beside the elevator ("too little to climb the rough
  stairway of perfection"); *NO.*; *She'd have hated Hale.*; *Okay, fine.* beside the trial of faith and the table of sinners; and,
  beside her two missionary "brothers," *Can a man and a woman be friends? Ask L.* He gives it to Lena at midnight on her feast;
  she will not let him erase a word ("Don't you dare"); on the duel's HEART path she writes back under *Easy for you.*: *Empty
  hands. —L.* (the Act of Oblation). **She keeps it.** After E08 Jack carries Hale's Williams again (margins still owed).
- **The Hopkins, erased.** Before it ships to Trumbull (Thu Oct 1, through Driftwood, with Hale's Housman), every margin comes out
  with the Pink Pearl — and **Lena erases *Show Lena* herself**, in the laundry room, in the freeze: "There. He can have it." (The
  small generous devastating gesture of E08; spend no other.) Danny's first letter, which lived inside it in the milk crate, moves
  into the Williams.
- **The Delgado laundry room** (basement): eight washers, three working; the dryers buzz like a game-show loss; the piano nobody
  admits to playing; Priya's laminate: LAUNDRY ROOM HOURS 7 A.M.–11 P.M. (THIS MEANS YOU.). Nonna's card in Jack's basket: *Whites
  in HOT. Darks in COLD. MEDALS OFF.* (E01) — Lena reads it and nearly laughs, in the freeze, and doesn't.
- **Lupe's "para los novios."** Lupe gives free horchata to couples; she gives Jack and Lena two; they say "We're friends" at the
  same time; "Claro."
- **Thomas the crab** (E08): a *Pagurus* hermit crab on loan from the Marine Lab touch tank, in a Tupperware of seawater, named for
  the First Way. Back in the tank by noon Saturday.
- **Gloria's people-ratings, E08:** Becca, sight unseen, from Jack's description: **"Apple. That's the one you bring home to your
  mother."** Then: "What happened to the pecan?" "She has a boyfriend." "Hm."
- **Bishop Berkeley** (Hale's deaf greyhound, named for the idealist: *esse est percipi*) on page at last (E08, the Blessing); the
  frayed leash from Hale's office door.
